But
Q(\Delta^{\bullet}z)\to Q(\tilde{z}^{\bullet})
is a levelwise split surjection and hence a fibration in the classical model structure on simplicial abelian groups; similarly for the right-hand column prior to taking
C_{2}
-homotopy fixed points. The total homotopy fibre of the cube

(2.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{Q}(\Delta^{\bullet}z,\Delta^{\bullet}z)^{\hct}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{Q}(\tilde{z}^{\bullet},\tilde{z}^{\bullet})^{\hct}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q(\Delta^{\bullet}z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q(\tilde{z}^{\bullet})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{Q}(\Delta^{\bullet}z,\tilde{z}^{\bullet})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{Q}(\tilde{z}^{\bullet},\tilde{z}^{\bullet})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{Q}(\Delta^{\bullet}z,\tilde{z}^{\bullet})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{Q}(\tilde{z}^{\bullet},\tilde{z}^{\bullet})}

identifies with (
2.10
), since the bottom face is trivially cartesian, and by commuting homotopy limits, we may compute this as the homotopy fibre of the map between the respective total homotopy fibres of the front and back faces; by Lemma
2.5.3
applied to the quadratic functors
Q
and
\Delta^{*}B_{Q}
, this is

\hofib\left(Q(z)\to B_{Q}(z,z)^{\fct}\right)\simeq\ker\left(Q(z)\to B_{Q}(z,z)^{\fct}\right)=L_{Q}(z).

Note that the polarisation
B
of
\Delta^{*}B_{Q}

B(u,v)=B_{Q}(u,v)\oplus B_{Q}(v,u),

where the
C_{2}
-action swaps the factors, and the square corresponding to the setup of Lemma
2.5.3
is

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{Q}(\Delta^{\bullet}z,\Delta^{\bullet}z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{Q}(\tilde{z}^{\bullet},\tilde{z}^{\bullet})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{Q}(\Delta^{\bullet}z,\tilde{z}^{\bullet})\oplus B_{Q}(\tilde{z}^{\bullet},\Delta^{\bullet}z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{Q}(\tilde{z}^{\bullet},\tilde{z}^{\bullet})\oplus B_{Q}(\tilde{z}^{\bullet},\tilde{z}^{\bullet}),}

which upon taking
C_{2}
-homotopy fixed points gives

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{Q}(\Delta^{\bullet}z,\Delta^{\bullet}z)^{\hct}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{Q}(\tilde{z}^{\bullet},\tilde{z}^{\bullet})^{\hct}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{Q}(\Delta^{\bullet}z,\tilde{z}^{\bullet})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{Q}(\tilde{z}^{\bullet},\tilde{z}^{\bullet}).}

The same argument applied to the other two columns and the bottom row of (
2.9
) gives that these are homotopy fibre sequences, since
\tilde{x}^{\bullet}\to\tilde{y}^{\bullet}\to\tilde{z}^{\bullet}
is again levelwise a split Frobenius conflation. It then follows that the middle row is a homotopy fibre sequence of simplicial abelian groups, i.e.
L_{\qdb}
sends conflations to fibre sequences of simplicial abelian groups, so by
[
HTT
, Th. 4.2.4.1]

\Lambda_{\qdb}
sends exact sequences in
L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})
to fibre sequences in
\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz)
.
∎

Remark 2.5.5
.

In fact, each column and the bottom row of (
2.9
) is a fibre sequence in
\mathrm{D}(\zz)
; the failure of
\Lambda_{\qdb}
to be 1-excisive when considered as a functor valued in
\mathrm{D}(\zz)
is thus controlled by the failure of the top row of (
2.9
) to be right exact in
\Ab
.

As a consequence of the discussion prior to Lemma
2.5.2
, we see that:

Corollary 2.5.6
.

The functor
\qdb:L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz)
is reduced and 2-excisive.

Postcomposing with the inclusion
\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{D}(\zz)
, we obtain by abuse of notation a functor

\qdb:L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\mathrm{D}(\zz),

which is reduced but in general fails spectacularly to be 2-excisive. Recall from
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
, Cons. 1.1.2.6]
that for a stable
\infty
-category
\cal{C}
, the inclusion

i:\fun_{*}(\cal{C}^{\op},\mathrm{D}(\zz))\hookrightarrow\fun_{*}^{2\mathrm{-exc}}(\cal{C}^{\op},\mathrm{D}(\zz))

admits a left adjoint
P_{2}
, the 2-excisive approximation, where
\fun_{*}
denotes the full subcategory of reduced functors. As demonstrated in the following proposition, when applied to a 2-excisive
\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz)
-valued functor, this is in some sense a universal 2-excisive delooping. Write
\fun^{\mathrm{q}}:=\fun_{*}^{2\mathrm{-exc}}
, and
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q:=P_{2}\qdb
. We have adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fun_{*}(\cal{C}^{\op},\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fun_{*}(\cal{C}^{\op},\mathrm{D}(\zz))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fun^{\mathrm{q}}(\cal{C}^{\op},\mathrm{D}(\zz))}

\scriptstyle\vdash

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{*}}

\scriptstyle\vdash

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tau_{\geq 0})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

where we write
j:\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{D}(\zz)
for the inclusion, left adjoint to
\tau_{\geq 0}
. Pointwise truncation sends 2-excisive
\mathrm{D}(\zz)
-valued functors to 2-excisive
\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz)
-valued functors, and so this restricts to an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fun^{\mathrm{q}}(\cal{C}^{\op},\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fun^{\mathrm{q}}(\cal{C}^{\op},\mathrm{D}(\zz)).}

\scriptstyle\vdash

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{2}j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\geq 0,*}i}

Proposition 2.5.7
.

For any stable
\infty
-category
\cal{C}
, the above is an adjoint equivalence. In particular, for any complicial exact form category with weak equivalences
(\cal{E},Q,w,\D,\eta)
, the natural map
\qdb\to\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q
truncates to an equivalence
\qdb\xrightarrow{\simeq}\tau_{\geq 0}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q
of functors
L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz)
.

Proof.

Suppose given some
\text{\char 21\relax}:\cal{C}^{\op}\to\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz)

2
-excisive, the unit

\text{\char 21\relax}\to\tau_{\geq 0,*}iP_{2}j_{*}\text{\char 21\relax}

is given pointwise by

\text{\char 21\relax}(x)\to\tau_{\geq 0}P_{2}j\text{\char 21\relax}(x)\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}\varinjlim_{n}T_{2}^{n}j\text{\char 21\relax}(x),

and since
\tau_{\geq 0}
commutes with filtered colimits, to show this is an equivalence it suffices to show that
\tau_{\geq 0}T_{2}^{\mathrm{D}(\zz)}j\simeq T_{2}^{\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz)}\tau_{\geq 0}j\simeq T_{2}^{\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz)}
, where the superscript indicates where the fibre is taken. But

T_{2}\text{\char 21\relax}(x)=\Omega\fib(\text{\char 21\relax}(\Omega x)\to B_{\text{\char 21\relax}}(\Omega x,\Omega x)),

and
\tau_{\geq 0}
, as a right adjoint, preserves fibre sequences. The left adjoint
P_{2}j_{*}
is then fully faithful, and so it suffices to show that
\tau_{\geq 0_{*}}
is conservative; this follows from
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
, Lem. 1.1.25]
.
∎

Remark 2.5.8
.

We have a canonical natural transformation
Q\Rightarrow\qdb\Rightarrow\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q
, with the first map given by the inclusion of
0
-simplices, and the second the unit of the adjunction
P_{2}\dashv i
. Accordingly, there are 2-cells in
\cat_{\infty}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{E}^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{w}(\cal{E})^{\op}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces cQ}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\qdb}

Writing
Q_{\mathrm{nd}}\subset Q
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q_{\mathrm{nd}}\subset\Omega^{\infty}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q
for the subfunctors of nondegenerate forms, this restricts to a natural transformation

Q_{\mathrm{nd}}\Rightarrow\gamma^{*}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q_{\mathrm{nd}}.

Write
B_{\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q}
for the polarisation of
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q
, given objectwise by

B_{\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q}(x,y):=\fib\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q(x\oplus y)\to\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q(x)\oplus\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q(y)\right),

and
\D:L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})^{\op}\to L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})
for the functor induced by
\D:\cal{E}^{\op}\to\cal{E}
under localisation (recall that
\D
preserves Frobenius equivalences). Recall that mapping spaces in any stable
\infty
-category
\cal{C}
canonically enhance to mapping spectra, via
[
HA
, Cor. 1.4.2.23]
: the functor
\Omega^{\infty}:\Sp\to\cal{S}
induces an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\Omega^{\infty}_{*}:\fun^{\mathrm{ex}}(\cal{C},\Sp)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\fun^{\mathrm{lex}}(\cal{C},\cal{S}),

where we write
\fun^{\mathrm{ex}}
for the subcategory of exact functors, and
\fun^{\mathrm{lex}}
for the subcategory of left-exact (finite limit preserving) functors. For
x\in\cal{C}
, write
\map_{\cal{C}}(x,-)
for the functor corresponding under this equivalence to
\Map_{\cal{C}}(x,-)
. This assignment is natural in
x
and accordingly assembles into a mapping spectrum bifunctor,
\map_{\cal{C}}:\cal{C}^{\op}\times\cal{C}\to\Sp
. In our case,
L_{w}(\cal{E})
is
\zz
-linear, and we have a mapping bifunctor

L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})^{\op}\times L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})\to\mathrm{D}(\zz).

Recall that we write
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q:=P_{2}\qdb:L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\mathrm{D}(\zz)
.

Proposition 2.5.9
.

There are equivalences

B_{\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q}(x,y)\simeq\map_{L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})}(x,\D(y)),

in
\mathrm{D}_{\zz}
, natural in
x
and
y
.

Proof.

Recall that the 2-excisive approximation
P_{2}\qdb
is computed pointwise in
\mathrm{D}(\zz)
as the sequential colimit

P_{2}\qdb(x)\simeq\colim(\qdb(x)\to T_{2}\qdb(x)\to T_{2}^{2}\qdb(x)\to\dots),

where
T_{2}\qdb(x)=\Omega\fib\left(\qdb(\Omega x)\to B_{\qdb}(\Omega x,\Omega x)\right)
. We compute

B_{T_{2}\qdb}(x,y)\simeq\Omega\kern 1.0pt\mathrm{fibt}\left(\hbox to284.91pt{\vbox to60.55pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 142.45276pt\lower-30.27565pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\displaystyle\simeq\Omega\kern 1.0pt\fib\left(B_{\qdb}(\Omega x,\Omega y)\xrightarrow{\Delta}B_{\qdb}(\Omega x,\Omega y)\oplus B_{\qdb}(\Omega y,\Omega x)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\Omega^{2}B_{\qdb}(\Omega x,\Omega y)\simeq\Omega^{2}\Map_{\Delta}(\Omega x,\Sigma\D(y)),

using that
\D(\Omega y))\simeq\Sigma\D(y))
. Inductively, we have

B_{T_{2}^{n}\qdb}(x,y)\simeq\Omega^{2n}B_{\qdb}(\Omega^{n}x,\Sigma^{n}\D(y))\simeq\Omega^{2n}B_{\qdb}(\Omega^{2n}x,\D(y)),

and in the colimit,

B_{\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q}(x,y)\simeq P_{1}(\Map(x,-))\D(y)),

for
P_{1}
the
1
-excisive approximation of Appendix
B.3
. But this is precisely the mapping spectrum
\map(x,\D(y))
in
L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})
: for
x\in\cal{E}
and an exact functor
F:L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})\to\mathrm{D}(\zz)
we have a string of equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{Nat}_{\fun^{\mathrm{ex}}(L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E}),\mathrm{D}(\zz))}\left(\map(x,-),F\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{Nat}_{\fun^{\mathrm{lex}}(L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E}),\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz))}\left(\Map(x,-),\tau_{\geq 0}F\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{Nat}_{\fun(L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E}),\mathrm{D}(\zz))}(\Map(x,-),F)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{Nat}_{\fun^{\mathrm{ex}}(L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E}),\mathrm{D}(\zz))}(P_{1}\Map(x,-),F).

∎

Recall
[
CDH$ˆ+$II
, §1.1]
that the Verdier quotient
\pi:L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})\to L_{w}(\cal{E})
is an exact functor of stable
\infty
-categories, sitting in a fibre-cofibre sequence

L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E}^{w})\to L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})\xrightarrow{\pi}L_{w}(\cal{E}),

\cal{E}^{w}\subset\cal{E}
the full subcategory of
w
-acyclic objects, which is closed under retracts. By
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
, Lem. 1.4.1(iii)]
, left Kan extension along an exact functor preserves preserves quadraticity (in the stable setting), and so writing
\mathbf{R}Q:=\pi_{!}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q
, the pair
(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)
is again the data of a hermitian
\infty
-category.

Theorem 2.5.10
.

For
(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta,w)
a complicial exact category with weak equivalences and strong duality, the pair
(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)
is the data of a Poincaré
\infty
-category. Moreover, for each
x\in\cal{E}
,
\mathbf{R}Q
satisfies

\tau_{\geq 0}\mathbf{R}Q(\gamma(x))\simeq\underset{J_{x}^{\op}}{\colim}\ Q,

for
J_{x}\subset(\cal{E}\downarrow x)
the full subcategory spanned by trivial deflations over
x
.

Proof.

Suppose firstly that
w
is the class of Frobenius equivalences. In this case,
\mathbf{R}Q=\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q
is reduced and 2-excisive by Corollary
2.5.6
, with nondegenerate polarisation by Proposition
2.5.9
; it remains to show that the induced duality
\D:L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})^{\op}\to L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})
is an equivalence. Since the unit
\id_{\cal{E}}\Rightarrow\D\D^{\op}
is a Frobenius equivalence by assumption, the induced functor
L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})^{\op}\to L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})
is an equivalence.

For the general case, we note that the polarisation
B_{\mathbf{R}Q}\simeq(\pi_{!}\times\pi_{!})B_{\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q}
is nondegenerate by the formula for mapping spaces in a Verdier quotient
[
NS18
, Th. I.3.3]
, and that the induced duality
\D:L_{w}(\cal{E})^{\op}\to L_{w}(\cal{E})
is an equivalence since again the unit and counit are natural weak equivalences. The last claim then follows from Proposition
A.1.15
(or Proposition
2.4.7
if
w=w_{\mathrm{Frob}}
), since the truncation
\tau_{\geq 0}
commutes with the filtered colimit appearing in the formula for left Kan extension along
L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})\to L_{w}(\cal{E})
.
∎

Remark 2.5.11
.

If the double dual identification
\eta
on
\cal{E}
is a natural Frobenius equivalence
4
4

4

This is the case for instance for the exact form category
(\chb(\cal{E}),Q_{\mathrm{ch}},\mathbf{qis},\D,\eta)
extended from
(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta)
, the claim for general
w
follows immediately from that for
w_{\mathrm{Frob}}
: by
[
CDH$ˆ+$II
, Ex. 1.1.7]
, for
(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
a Poincaré category and
p:\cal{C}\to\cal{D}
a Verdier projection, the pair
(\cal{D},p_{!}\text{\char 21\relax})
is Poincaré if and only if
\ker(p)
is closed under the duality
\D_{\text{\char 21\relax}}
. Note for this that
\cal{E}^{w}\subset\cal{E}
is closed under the (exact) duality functor.

2.6.
Functoriality

Write
\excat
for the category of small exact categories and exact functors, and
\wcompcat
for the category of small complicial exact categories with weak equivalences and exact functors between them. Recall from Remark
2.1.8
the definition of
\wcompformcat
as the subcategory of the Grothendieck construction on the functor

\wcompcat^{\op}\to\mathrm{CAT}_{1},\quad(\cal{E},w)\mapsto\fun^{\mathrm{q}}(\cal{E})

spanned by those tuples
(\cal{E},w,Q)
for which the polarisation
B_{Q}(-,-)\cong\Hom(-,\D(-))
induces a strong duality
(\D,\eta)
on
\cal{E}
, with maps the nonsingular exact form functors. There are functors

\displaystyle\excat\to\wcompcat\xrightarrow{L_{(-)}}\cat_{\infty},

\displaystyle\cal{E}\mapsto(\chb(\cal{E}),\mathbf{qis}),(\cal{E},w)\mapsto L_{w}(\cal{E})

where the latter is the composite
\wcompcat\subset\relcat\to\cat_{\infty}
, which by Proposition
A.1.10
factors through the inclusion
\cat^{\mathrm{st}}_{\infty}\subset\cat_{\infty}
. The functor
\relcat\to\cat_{\infty}
is the composite

\relcat\xrightarrow{N}\sset^{+}\to\cat_{\infty}

for
\sset^{+}
the category of marked simplicial sets with marked model structure
[
HTT
, §3]
, with underlying
\infty
-category
\cat_{\infty}
, and
\relcat
the ordinary category of pairs
(\cal{C},W)
, for
\cal{C}
a category and
W\subset\cal{C}
a wide subcategory, with maps
(\cal{C},W)\to(\cal{D},V)
functors
f:\cal{C}\to\cal{D}
with
f(W)\subset V
. The functor
N
sends
(\cal{C},W)
to
(N(\cal{C}),N(W))
, and the second is the canonical localisation, which can be modelled for instance as fibrant replacement in
\sset^{+}

[
Hin16
, §1.1]
.

Proposition 2.6.1
.

There is a canonical functor
\wcompformcat\to\cat^{p}_{\infty}
, sending a complicial exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality
(\cal{E},Q,w,\D,\eta)
to the Poincaré
\infty
-category
(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)
defined above.

Proof.

We have functors

\displaystyle(\wcompcat)^{\op}\to\mathrm{CAT}_{1}\to\mathrm{CAT}_{\infty},

\displaystyle\quad(\cal{E},w)\mapsto\fun^{\mathrm{qlex}}(\cal{E}^{\op},\Ab),

\displaystyle\left(\cat^{\mathrm{st}}_{\infty}\right)^{\op}\to\mathrm{CAT}_{\infty},

\displaystyle\quad\cal{C}\mapsto\fun^{\mathrm{q}}(\cal{C}^{\op},\mathrm{D}(\zz)),

where
\fun^{\mathrm{qlex}}
and
\fun^{\mathrm{q}}
denote the full subcategories of the respective functor categories
\fun(\cal{E}^{\op},\Ab)
and
\fun(\cal{C}^{\op},\mathrm{D}(\zz))
spanned by quadratic left-exact resp. quadratic functors. Recall that by definition
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
, Def. 1.2.1]

\int_{\cat_{\infty}^{\mathrm{st}}}\fun^{\mathrm{q}}=\cat_{\infty}^{h},

the
\infty
-category of hermitian
\infty
-categories, and write
\cat^{h,w}_{1,\mathrm{comp}}
for the unstraightening
\int_{\wcompcat}\fun^{\mathrm{qlex}}
. The localisation functor
L_{(-)}:\wcompcat\to\cat_{\infty}^{\mathrm{st}}
induces a map of right fibrations

\int_{\wcompcat}L_{(-)}^{*}\fun^{\mathrm{q}}\to\cat^{h}_{\infty},

and composing with the functor
\cat^{h,w}_{1,\mathrm{comp}}\to\int_{\wcompcat}L_{(-)}^{*}\fun^{\mathrm{q}}
induced on unstraightenings by the composite

\fun^{\mathrm{qlex}}(\cal{E}^{\op},\Ab)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{!}(-)^{\Delta^{\bullet}}}\fun^{\mathrm{q}}(L_{w}(\cal{E})^{\op},\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz))\xrightarrow{P_{2}}\fun^{\mathrm{q}}(L_{w}(\cal{E})^{\op},\mathrm{D}(\zz))

for
\gamma:\cal{E}\to L_{w}(\cal{E})
the Dwyer-Kan localisation, we obtain a functor
\cat^{h,w}_{1,\mathrm{comp}}\to\cat^{h}_{\infty}
, which on objects sends
(\cal{E},Q)\mapsto(L_{w}(\cal{E}),P_{2}\gamma_{!}\qdb=:\mathbf{R}Q)
. We have a non-full embedding
\wcompformcat\subset\cat^{h,w}_{1,\mathrm{comp}}
. Given a tuple
(\cal{E},Q,w,\D,\eta)
in
\wcompformcat
, the pair
(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)
is a Poincaré
\infty
-category by
2.5.10
, and given a nonsingular form functor

(f,\varphi_{q},\varphi):(\cal{E},Q,w,\D,\eta)\to(\cal{E}^{\prime},Q^{\prime},w^{\prime},\D^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}),

the induced functor
F:L_{w}(\cal{E})\to L_{w^{\prime}}(\cal{E}^{\prime})
on localisations is then duality preserving since
\varphi
is a natural weak equivalence.
∎

2.7.
Poincaré structures on derived
\infty
-categories

We now specialise the above to the case of form exact categories of chain complexes. Analogously to the story for
K
-theory, the passage from an exact category to a stable
\infty
-category goes via the (bounded) derived
\infty
-category
\db(\cal{E})
, as recalled in Appendix
A.2
. Fix an exact form category with strong duality
(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta)
. Recall from
2.1
that the exact embedding
\cal{E}\hookrightarrow\chb(\cal{E})
enhances to an exact form functor of exact form categories with weak equivalences

(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta,\mathbf{iso})\to(\chb(\cal{E}),Q_{\mathrm{ch}},\D,\eta,\mathbf{qis}),

where the value of
Q_{\mathrm{ch}}
at a bounded chain complex
X
is the abelian group of pairs
(\xi,\varphi)
, for
\xi\in Q(X_{0})
with
d_{1}^{\bullet}(\xi)=0\in Q(X_{1})
, and
\varphi:X\to\D(X)
a symmetric form satisfying
\varphi_{0}=\rho(\xi)
. We denote by
\tau
and
\rho
also the corresponding transfer and restriction maps

\Hom_{\chb(\cal{E})}(X,X)_{\fct}\xrightarrow{\tau_{X}}Q_{\mathrm{ch}}(X)\xrightarrow{\rho_{X}}\Hom_{\chb(\cal{E})}(X,X)^{\fct}

for a bounded chain complex
X
. The exact category
\chb(\cal{E})
has a canonical complicial structure given by an extension of the
\projz
-action on
\cal{E}
arising from additivity, with corresponding Frobenius exact structure consisting of degreewise split monomorphisms and epimorphisms, and Frobenius equivalences the chain homotopy equivalences. The construction of the previous section then gives rise to a functor
Q_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\Delta^{\bullet}}:\chb(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\sab
sending chain homotopies to simplicial homotopies, and we have a diagram of functors and natural transformations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{E}^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Ab}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bbDelta^{\op}\Ab.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chb(\cal{E})^{\op}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\cal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q_{\mathrm{ch}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\Delta^{\bullet}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{0}}

Lemma 2.7.1
.

\eta_{0}
is an isomorphism, as is the whiskered transformation

c\circ Q\stackrel{{\scriptstyle c_{\eta_{0}}}}{{\Rightarrow}}c\circ Q_{\mathrm{ch}}\circ\iota_{\cal{E}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\eta_{1})_{\iota_{\cal{E}}}}}{{\Rightarrow}}Q_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\Delta^{\bullet}}\circ\iota_{\cal{E}}.

Proof.

The first statement then follows from the definition of
Q_{\mathrm{ch}}
; for the second, for an object
x
of
\cal{E}
, consider the map

cQ(x)=cQ_{\mathrm{ch}}(x[0])\to Q_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\Delta^{\bullet}}(x[0])

induced by the unique map
\Delta^{\bullet}\to c\Delta^{0}
. The codegeneracies
s_{n}:\Delta^{n}x[0]\epi x[0]
induce a splitting

\Delta^{n}x[0]\cong\tilde{x}^{n}\oplus x[0]

for each
n
, where
\tilde{x}^{n}:=\ker(s_{n})
is contractible. For
x\in\cal{E}
and
i\in\zz
, write
D_{i}(x):=C\otimes x[i-1]
for the complex

\dots\to 0\to x=\joinrel=\joinrel=x\to 0\to\dots,

concentrated in degrees
i,i-1
.

Lemma 2.7.2
.

For each
n\geq 1
we have
\tilde{x}^{n}\cong\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq n}D_{i}\left(x^{\oplus\binom{n}{i}}\right)
.

Proof.

This follows from the analogous statement in
\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
, i.e. that

\tilde{\Delta}^{n}:=\ker\left(N\zz[\Delta^{n}]\epi N\zz[\Delta^{0}]\right)\cong\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq n}D_{i}\left(\zz^{\oplus\binom{n}{i}}\right).

The category of finitely generated projective
\zz
-modules is idempotent complete and hence so is
\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
, so the contractible chain complex
\tilde{\Delta}^{n}
splits as a finite direct sum of discs
D_{i}(\zz)
,
\tilde{\Delta}^{n}\cong\bigoplus_{i\in S}D_{i}(\zz^{n_{i}})
for some finite set
S\subset\mathbb{N}
and
n_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
. In degrees
0\leq i\leq n
,
\Delta^{n}
is isomorphic to
\zz[(\Delta^{n}_{i})^{\mathrm{nd}}]\cong\zz^{\binom{n+1}{i+1}}
and is 0 elsewhere, so by descending induction on the degree
0\leq i\leq n
, we have that the
i^{\mathrm{th}}
component of the direct sum is

D_{i}\left(\zz^{\oplus\sum_{i\leq j\leq n}(-1)^{j+i}\binom{n+1}{j+1}}\right)\cong D_{i}\left(\zz^{\oplus\binom{n}{i}}\right).

∎

Now for each
n\geq 1
we have

\displaystyle Q_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Delta^{n}x[0])

\displaystyle\cong Q_{\mathrm{ch}}\left(x[0]\oplus\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq n}D_{i}\left(x^{\oplus\binom{n}{i}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\cong Q_{\mathrm{ch}}(x[0])\oplus Q_{\mathrm{ch}}\left(\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq n}D_{i}\left(x^{\oplus\binom{n}{i}}\right)\right)\oplus\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq n}\Hom_{\chb(\cal{E})}\left(D_{i}\left(x^{\oplus\binom{n}{i}}\right),\D(x)[0]\right),

by quadraticity. The second summand evaluates to

Q_{\mathrm{ch}}\left(\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq n}D_{i}\left(x^{\oplus\binom{n}{i}}\right)\right)\cong\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq n}Q_{\mathrm{ch}}\left(D_{i}\left(x^{\oplus\binom{n}{i}}\right)\right)\oplus\bigoplus_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\Hom_{\chb(\cal{E})}\left(D_{i}\left(x^{\oplus\binom{n}{i}}\right),\D\left(D_{j}\left(x^{\oplus\binom{n}{j}}\right)\right)\right)=0,

since

\Hom_{\chb(\cal{E})}(D_{i}(y),\D(D_{j}(z)))=\Hom_{\chb(\cal{E})}(D_{i}(y),D_{1-j}(\D(z)))=\begin{cases}\Hom_{\cal{E}}(y,\D(z)),&j=-i,-i+1,\\
0,&\text{ else,}\end{cases}

for each
y,z\in\cal{E}
, and

\displaystyle Q_{\mathrm{ch}}(D_{i}(y))=

\displaystyle\{(\xi,\varphi)\mid\xi\in Q(D_{i}(y)_{0}),\varphi\in\Hom_{\chb(\cal{E})}(D_{i}(y),\D(D_{i}(y)))^{\fct},\rho(\xi)=\varphi_{0},d_{1}^{\bullet}(\xi)=0\}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\begin{cases}Q(y),&i=0,\\
0,&\text{ else.}\end{cases}

Similarly, the third term evaluates to

\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq n}\Hom_{\chb(\cal{E})}\left(D_{i}\left(x^{\oplus\binom{n}{i}}\right),\D(x)[0]\right)=0,

since

\Hom_{\chb(\cal{E})}\left(D_{i}\left(x^{\oplus\binom{n}{i}}\right),\D(x)[0]\right)=\begin{cases}\Hom_{\chb(\cal{E})}\left(x,\D(x)\right),&i=0,\\
0,&\text{ else.}\end{cases}

The composite
Q_{\mathrm{ch}}(x[0])\to Q_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Delta^{n}x[0])\cong Q_{\mathrm{ch}}(\tilde{x}^{n})\oplus Q_{\mathrm{ch}}(x[0])\oplus\Hom_{\chb(\cal{E})}(\tilde{x}^{n},\D(x))
is

\begin{pmatrix}Q_{\mathrm{ch}}(\iota_{0})\\
Q_{\mathrm{ch}}(\iota_{1})\\
\tau(\D(\iota_{1})\rho(-)\iota_{0})\end{pmatrix}\circ Q_{\mathrm{ch}}(\pi_{1})=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\\
0\end{pmatrix},

and clearly this furnishes an isomorphism
Q(x)\cong Q_{\mathrm{ch}}(x[0])\cong Q_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Delta^{n}x[0])
. But the above map coincides with the degeneracy
Q_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\Delta^{0}}(x[0])\to Q_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\Delta^{n}}(x[0])
, and hence the levelwise isomorphism above assembles into an isomorphism of simplicial abelian groups

cQ(x)\cong\tqdb(x[0]).

∎

In the sequel we write
Q
for both the functor
\cal{E}^{\op}\to\Ab
and its extension to
\chb(\cal{E})
. By Corollary
2.5.6
, we have that
\qdb
descends to a
\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz)
-valued presheaf on the stable
\infty
-category
L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\chb(\cal{E}))=\kb(\cal{E})
which is 2-excisive, and admits an essentially unique 2-excisive
\mathrm{D}(\zz)
-valued delooping which we denote
\text{\char 21\relax}:\kb(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\mathrm{D}(\zz)
. In general, this delooping will be non-connective; we claim however that for coconnective complexes
X
the natural map
\qdb(X)\to\text{\char 21\relax}(X)
, the unit of the adjunction
P_{2}\dashv i
of §
2.5
, is an equivalence. Observe firstly that for such
X
, the mapping spectrum
\map(X,\D(X))
is connective, since
\D(X)
is concentrated in nonnegative degrees.
5
5

5

This is a consequence of the natural weight structure on
\kb(\cal{E})
; see
[
HS23
, Ex. 3.1.3(i)]
.
The fibre
F
of the map

\Omega\qdb(\Omega X)\to\Omega\tau_{\geq 0}\Map_{\Delta}(\Omega X,\D(\Omega X))

in
\mathrm{D}(\zz)
sits in a long exact sequence

\displaystyle\dots

\displaystyle\to\pi_{0}F\to\pi_{1}\qdb(\Omega X)\to\pi_{1}\Map(\Omega X,\D(\Omega X))\to\pi_{-1}F\to\pi_{0}\qdb(\Omega X)\to\dots

\displaystyle\dots

\displaystyle\to\pi_{0}\Map(\Omega X,\D(\Omega X))\to\pi_{-2}F\to\pi_{-1}\qdb(X)\to 0.

Now
\Map(\Omega X,\D(\Omega X))\simeq\Omega^{2}\Map(X,\D(X))
is 2-connective, and
\pi_{0}\qdb(\Omega X)=0
since
Q(\Omega X)=0
for coconnective
X
. Accordingly, the map
F\to\qdb(X)
is a
\pi_{*}
-isomorphism and hence an equivalence of spectra, and in the sequential colimit, we obtain
\qdb(X)\simeq P_{2}\qdb(X)
for coconnective complexes
X
. In particular,
\text{\char 21\relax}\mid_{\cal{E}^{\op}}\simeq HQ
, for
H:\Ab\to\mathrm{D}(\zz)
the Eilenberg-MacLane embedding.

Corollary 2.7.3
.

\text{\char 21\relax}:\kb(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\mathrm{D}(\zz)
is the essentially unique quadratic extension of
Q:\cal{E}^{\op}\to\Ab
. Moreover, the connective cover
\tau_{\geq 0}\text{\char 21\relax}(X)
satisfies

\tau_{\geq 0}\text{\char 21\relax}(X)\simeq\underset{J^{\op}_{X,\mathrm{Frob}}}{\colim}\ Q

in
\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz)
.

Proof.

By above,
˘
is a quadratic extension of
HQ
; uniqueness follows from
[
BGMN22
, Th. 2.19]
.
∎

Remark 2.7.4

(Cf.
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
, Rem. 4.2.19]
)
.

It can be shown that upon restriction to coconnective chain complexes, the derived functor of
Q
is given by the Dold-Puppe non-additive derived functor
[
DP58
]

\wchb(\cal{E})^{\op}\hookrightarrow(\bbDelta\cal{E})^{\op}\simeq\bbDelta^{\op}\cal{E}^{\op}\to\sab\to\mathrm{D}(\zz),

where the first functor is furnished by Dold-Kan, and is fully faithful in general (and upon replacing the source with all connective chain complexes, an equivalence for
\cal{E}
weakly idempotent complete; see for instance
[
HA
, Th. 1.2.3.7]
). A computation shows that for coconnective
X
there is an equivalence

\qdb(X)\simeq\underset{[n]\in\bbDelta^{\op}}{\colim}\ Q(X^{n}),

for
\underset{[n]\in\bbDelta}{\lim}\ X^{n}\simeq X
a cosimplicial resolution of
X
, and so for
X\in\wchb(\cal{E})
, we recover

\text{\char 21\relax}(X)\simeq\qdb(X).

Passage to the derived
\infty
-category of
\cal{E}
is then given by the Verdier projection
\pi:\kb(\cal{E})\to\db(\cal{E})
at the subcategory of acyclics
\acb(\cal{E})
; left Kan extending
˘
along
\pi
, we obtain a hermitian
\infty
-category
(\db(\cal{E}),\pi_{!}\text{\char 21\relax})
.

Theorem 2.7.5
.

For
(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta)
an exact form category with strong duality, the pair
(\db(\cal{E}),\pi_{!}\text{\char 21\relax})
is the data of a Poincaré
\infty
-category. Moreover, the derived functor
\pi_{!}\text{\char 21\relax}
satisfies

\tau_{\geq 0}\pi_{!}\text{\char 21\relax}(X)\simeq\underset{J_{X}^{\op}}{\colim}\ Q

in
\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz)
.

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
2.5.10
(and Remark
2.5.11
), in light of the fact that the duality on
\cal{E}
induces a strong duality on
\chb(\cal{E})
and hence on both
\kb(\cal{E})
and
\db(\cal{E})
.
∎

3.
Grothendieck-Witt spaces

In this section we construct a natural equivalence

\cal{GW}(\cal{E},Q,w)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\cal{GW}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q),

for
(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta,w)
a complicial exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality, and associated Poincaré category
(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)
. The argument builds on that of
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
, App. B.2]
: since the Grothendieck-Witt space in both the classical and Poincaré setting is constructed as the fibre of a map between realisations of certain simplicial spaces, it is enough to exhibit a levelwise equivalence between the latter. The comparison then reduces to an analysis of the underlying infinite loopspace of the right derived quadratic functor
\mathbf{R}Q
of the previous section. We begin in §
3.1
by recalling the constructions of the Grothendieck-Witt spaces in each setting. We then proceed in §
3.2
with a comparison of the spaces of nondegenerate forms associated to an exact form category and to a Poincaré
\infty
-category, which in §§
3.3
we extend to the hermitian
S_{\bullet}
- and
\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}
-constructions. The comparison then follows easily in §
3.4
.

3.1.
Grothendieck-Witt theory

3.1.1.
\cal{GW}
for exact categories

In this section we recall the construction of the Grothendieck-Witt space associated to an exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality; see
[
Sch21
, §6]
,
[
Sch24a
, §7]
for details.

To an exact form category with weak equivalences
(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta,w)
, we may associate the category of quadratic spaces
\wquad(\cal{E},Q,w)
. This is the Grothendieck construction on the functor
Q_{\mathrm{nd}}
of Remark
2.5.1
,

Q_{\mathrm{nd}}:w\cal{E}^{\op}\to\set\subset\cat,x\mapsto\{\xi\in Q(x)\mid\rho_{x}(\xi)\in w\cal{E}\},

classifying the right fibration
\wquad(\cal{E},Q,w)\to w\cal{E}
,
(x,q)\mapsto x
. Explicitly, objects are pairs
(x,\xi)
for
x
an object of
\cal{E}
and
\xi\in Q(x)
such that
\rho_{x}(\xi)\in\Hom_{\cal{E}}(x,\D(x))
is a weak equivalence, and maps
(x,\xi)\to(y,\zeta)
are weak equivalences
f:x\to y
in
\cal{E}
satisfying
f^{\bullet}(\zeta)=\xi
.

Suppose now given a poset
(\mathcal{P},\leq)
with strict duality
\mathcal{P}^{\op}\to\mathcal{P}
,
i\mapsto\overline{i}
. For an exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality
(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta,w)
, the category
\fun(\mathcal{P},\cal{E})
acquires the structure of an exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality as follows. The dual of a functor
X:\mathcal{P}\to\cal{E}
,
i\mapsto X_{i}
is

\D_{\mathcal{P}}(X):i\mapsto\D(X_{\overline{i}}),

with double dual identification given pointwise by
\eta
. A natural transformation
X\to Y
is a weak equivalence if it is so pointwise. A quadratic form on a functor
X:\mathcal{P}\to\cal{E}
is the data of a pair
((\xi_{i})_{i\leq\overline{i}},\varphi)
, for
(\xi_{i})_{i\leq\overline{i}}\in\prod_{i\leq\overline{i}}Q(X_{i})
and
\varphi:X\to\D_{\mathcal{P}}(X)
a symmetric form on
X
, satisfying
X_{i\leq j}^{\bullet}(\xi_{j})=\xi_{i}
for each pair
i,j
satisfying
i\leq j\leq\overline{j}\leq\overline{i}
, and
\rho_{X_{i}}(\xi_{i})=\varphi_{\overline{i}}\circ X_{i\leq\overline{i}}
. The set
Q_{\mathcal{P}}(X)
of such forms is an abelian group with
(\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\leq\overline{i}},\varphi)+(\{\eta_{j}\}_{j\leq\overline{j}},\psi)=(\{\xi_{i}+\zeta_{i}\}_{i\leq\overline{i}},\varphi+\psi)
. Informally, a form on
X
is a compatible family of pointwise forms indexed by the subposet of elements
i\in\mathcal{P}
with
i\leq\overline{i}
, and a symmetric form on
X
compatible with the family of symmetric forms coming from the
\xi_{i}
; we shall see below that in the case
\mathcal{P}=\mathrm{Ar}([n])
, the induced structure of an exact form category on
S_{n}(\cal{E})
is such that the map
\varphi
is determined uniquely by the
\xi_{i}
. Functoriality is inherited from that of
Q
on
\cal{E}
: for
\theta:X\to Y
a natural transformation, we define

Q_{\mathcal{P}}(Y)\to Q_{\mathcal{P}}(X),\quad(\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\leq\overline{i}},\varphi)\mapsto(\{\theta^{\bullet}(\xi_{i})\}_{i\leq\overline{i}},\D(\theta)\varphi\theta).

Associated to each functor
X
is the
C_{2}
-Mackey functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{Nat}(X,\D_{\mathcal{P}}(X))\xrightarrow{\tau_{X}}Q(X)\xrightarrow{\rho_{X}}\mathrm{Nat}(X,\D_{\mathcal{P}}(X))

\displaystyle\tau_{X}:\varphi\mapsto(\{\tau(\varphi_{\overline{i}}\circ X_{i\leq\overline{i}})\}_{i\leq\overline{i}},\varphi+\sigma(\varphi)),

\displaystyle\rho_{X}:(\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\leq\overline{i}},\varphi)\mapsto\varphi,

from which one may check that
Q_{\mathcal{P}}
is quadratic by the criteria of
[
Sch21
, Lem. A.10(ii)]
.

Recall that the category
\mathrm{Ar}([n])
has a unique strict duality
(i\leq j)\mapsto(n-j,n-i)
; for
\cal{E}
as above, the category
\fun(\mathrm{Ar}([n]),\cal{E})
thus inherits the structure of an exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality. To ease notation, we write
X^{i\leq j}_{k\leq l}
for the image under
X
of the map
(i\leq j)\leq(k\leq l)
, and
Q_{n}:=Q_{\mathrm{Ar}([n])}
. Restricting to the subcategory of functors
X:\mathrm{Ar}([n])\to\cal{E}
such that
X_{i\leq i}\cong 0
, and such that for each
0\leq i\leq j\leq k\leq n
the sequence

X_{i\leq j}\mono X_{i\leq k}\epi X_{j\leq k}

is a conflation in
\cal{E}
, we obtain an exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality
(S_{n}(\cal{E}),Q,\D,\eta,w)
. Denote by
\mathcal{P}_{n}\subset\mathrm{Ar}([n])
the subposet of arrows
(i\leq j)
satisfying

(i\leq j)\leq\overline{(i\leq j)}=(n-j\leq n-i),

i.e. with
i+j\leq n
. A quadratic form on
X\in S_{n}(\cal{E})
is a pair
(\{\xi_{i\leq j}\}_{i+j\leq n},\varphi)
, with the family
(\xi_{i\leq j})
an object in the limit
\lim_{(i\leq j)\in\mathcal{P}_{n}^{\op}}Q
, satisfying

\rho_{X_{i\leq j}}(\xi_{i\leq j})=\varphi_{n-j\leq n-i}\circ X^{i\leq j}_{(n-j\leq n-i)}.

In particular we have
\rho_{X_{0\leq n}}(\xi_{0\leq n})=\varphi_{0\leq n}
; as noted in
[
HS23
, Rem. 8.2.5(i)]
,
\varphi
is determined by the
\xi_{i\leq j}
: for each
0\leq i\leq n
we have a map of conflations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{0\leq i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{0\leq n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i\leq n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\D(X_{n-i\leq n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\D(X_{0\leq n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\D(X_{0\leq n-i}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{0\leq i}_{0\leq n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{0\leq i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{0\leq n}_{i\leq n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{0\leq n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{i\leq n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\D(X^{0\leq n}_{n-i\leq n})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\D(X^{0\leq n-i}_{0\leq n})}

from which we see that
\varphi_{0\leq i}
and
\varphi_{i\leq n}
are determined by
\varphi_{0\leq n}
, and repeating this with the map of conflations associated with
X_{0\leq i}\mono X_{0\leq j}\epi X_{i\leq j}
, similarly for
\varphi_{i\leq j}
. Accordingly, we have an injection

Q_{n}(X)\hookrightarrow\lim_{(i\leq j)\in\mathcal{P}_{n}^{\op}}Q\circ X^{\op},\quad((\xi_{i})_{i},\varphi)\mapsto(\xi_{i})_{i}.

Construction 3.1.1
.

Contravariant naturality of the
S_{\bullet}
-construction yields a simplicial exact category
S_{\bullet}(\cal{E})
. The simplicial structure maps of
S_{\bullet}(\cal{E})
are however not compatible with the dualities, but are if we consider the edgewise subdivision
S^{e}_{\bullet}(\cal{E})
, obtained by precomposing with the functor
\mathrm{sd}:\bbDelta^{\op}\to\bbDelta^{\op}
,
[n]\mapsto[n]^{\op}\star[n]\cong[2n+1]
(this has the effect of symmetrising faces and degeneracies). We thus obtain a simplicial exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality, and a functor

S_{\bullet}:\wformcat\to\bbDelta^{\op}\kern-1.0pt\wformcat,\quad(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta,w)\mapsto(S^{e}_{\bullet}(\cal{E}),Q^{e}_{\bullet},\D^{e}_{\bullet},\eta^{e}_{\bullet},w).

Postcomposing with
\wquad
and taking nerves, we have a simplicial space

[p]\mapsto\left|\wquad(S_{2p+1}(\cal{E}),Q_{2p+1},w)\right|

The forgetful functors

\wquad(S_{n}\cal{E},Q_{n},w)\to wS_{n}(\cal{E})

induce a map of simplicial spaces

[p]\mapsto\left(\left|\wquad(S_{2p+1}(\cal{E}),Q_{2p+1},w)\right|\to\left|wS_{2p+1}(\cal{E})\right|\to\left|wS_{p}(\cal{E})\right|\right),

where the last map is induced levelwise by the natural transformation
1_{\bbDelta}\Rightarrow\mathrm{sd}
,
[n]\subset[n]^{\op}\star[n]
,
i\mapsto i
. The Grothendieck-Witt space
[
Sch21
, Def. 6.3]
is defined to be the homotopy fibre of the corresponding map of geometric realisations

\cal{GW}(\cal{E},Q,w)\to\left|\wquad(S^{e}_{\bullet}(\cal{E}),Q^{e}_{\bullet},w)\right|\to\left|wS_{\bullet}(\cal{E})\right|.

Remark 3.1.2
.

The constructions in
[
Sch21
]
and
[
Sch24a
]
make use of the functor

\mathrm{sd}_{0}:\bbDelta\to\bbDelta,\quad[n]\mapsto[n]^{\op}\star[n],

while the authors of
[
CDH$ˆ+$II
]
work with the
\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}
-construction corresponding to the subdivision

\mathrm{sd}_{1}:\bbDelta\to\bbDelta,\quad[n]\mapsto[n]\star[n]^{\op}.

Here we write
(-)^{\op}:\bbDelta^{\op}\to\bbDelta^{\op}
for the involution
[n]\mapsto[n]^{\op}
,
\theta\mapsto\theta^{\op}
, where
[n]^{\op}=(n^{\prime}<(n-1)^{\prime}<\dots<1^{\prime}<0^{\prime})
and
\theta^{\op}:[m]^{\op}\to[n]^{\op}
is
i^{\prime}\mapsto\theta(i)^{\prime}
. Given a map
\theta:[m]\to[n]
,
\mathrm{sd}_{0}(\theta)
(resp.
\mathrm{sd}_{1}(\theta)
) is the map
\theta^{\op}\star\theta
(resp.
\theta\star\theta^{\op}
).
There is an identification of simplicial spaces

\left([n]\mapsto\left|\wquad(S_{\mathrm{sd}_{0}[n]}(\cal{E}),Q_{\mathrm{sd}_{0}[n]})\right|\right)=\left([n]\mapsto\left|\wquad(S_{\mathrm{sd}_{1}[n]}(\cal{E}),Q_{\mathrm{sd}_{1}[n]})\right|\right)^{\op},

where the opposite of a simplicial space
X
is given by precomposition with
(-)^{\op}
. Accordingly, since
(-)^{\op}
is cofinal (see for instance
[
Bar13
, §2]
), we have equivalences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left|\wquad(\mathrm{sd}_{0}^{*}(S_{\bullet}(\cal{E}),Q_{\bullet}))\right|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left|wS_{\bullet}(\cal{E})\right|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left|\wquad(\mathrm{sd}_{1}^{*}(S_{\bullet}(\cal{E}),Q_{\bullet}))\right|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left|wS_{\bullet}(\cal{E})^{\op}\right|}

\scriptstyle\simeq

\scriptstyle\simeq

and an induced equivalence on fibres.

3.1.2.
\cal{GW}
for stable
\infty
-categories

Fix a Poincaré
\infty
-category
(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
; we have a presheaf of spaces
\Omega^{\infty}\text{\char 21\relax}
on
\cal{C}
, associating to each
x\in\cal{C}
the space of forms on
x
, the cartesian unstraightening of which is denoted
\mathrm{He}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
. Call a form
\xi\in\Omega^{\infty}\text{\char 21\relax}(x)

nondegenerate
if the symmetric form associated via the natural transformation
\text{\char 21\relax}(x)\to B_{\text{\char 21\relax}}(x,x)
is an equivalence. There is a subfunctor inclusion

(3.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{\char 21\relax}_{\mathrm{nd}}\subset\Omega^{\infty}\text{\char 21\relax}:\cal{C}^{\op}\to\cal{S},}

with
\text{\char 21\relax}_{\mathrm{nd}}(x)
the subspace spanned by the nondegenerate forms on
x
, i.e. the pullback

(3.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{\char 21\relax}_{\mathrm{nd}}(x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}\text{\char 21\relax}(x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\cal{C}^{\simeq}}(x,\D(x))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\cal{C}}(x,\D(x)).}

The inclusion
\text{\char 21\relax}_{\mathrm{nd}}\subset\Omega^{\infty}\text{\char 21\relax}
gives rise to a map of right fibrations over
\cal{C}

\int_{\cal{C}}\text{\char 21\relax}_{\mathrm{nd}}\to\mathrm{He}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}),

and pulling back along the inclusion
\cal{C}^{\simeq}\subset\cal{C}
, naturality of the straightening-unstraightening equivalence yields a square in
\cat_{\infty}

(3.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int_{\cal{C}^{\simeq}}\text{\char 21\relax}_{\mathrm{nd}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{He}(\cal{C}^{\simeq},\text{\char 21\relax})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int_{\cal{C}}\text{\char 21\relax}_{\mathrm{nd}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{He}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})}

commuting up to canonical equivalence.

Lemma 3.1.3
.

Suppose given a functor
F:\cal{D}^{\op}\to\cal{S}
classifying the right fibration
\int_{\cal{D}}F
, and denote by
\iota:\cal{D}^{\simeq}\subset\cal{D}
the inclusion of the groupoid core. Then there is a natural equivalence of spaces

\left(\int_{\cal{D}}F\right)^{\simeq}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\iota^{*}\int_{\cal{D}}F=\int_{\cal{D}^{\simeq}}\iota^{*}F.

Proof.

That
\iota^{*}\int_{\cal{D}}F
is a space follows from the fact that a right fibration over a Kan complex is a Kan fibration. The pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{*}\int_{\cal{D}}F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int_{\cal{D}}F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{D}^{\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

induces an essentially unique map
\left(\int_{\cal{D}}F\right)^{\simeq}\to\iota^{*}\int_{\cal{D}}F
, and given a space
X
, we observe that this induces an equivalence

\displaystyle\Map_{\cal{S}}(X,\left(\int_{\cal{D}}F\right)^{\simeq})\simeq

\displaystyle\Map_{\cat_{\infty}}(X,\int_{\cal{D}}F)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Map_{\cat_{\infty}}(X,\int_{\cal{D}}F)\times_{{\Map_{\cat_{\infty}}(X,\cal{D})}}\Map_{\cat_{\infty}}(X,\cal{D}^{\simeq})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Map_{\cal{S}}(X,\iota^{*}\int_{\cal{D}}F),

since the composite
X\to\int_{\cal{D}}F\to\cal{D}
necessarily factors through
\iota:\cal{D}^{\simeq}\hookrightarrow\cal{D}
.
∎

The upper-right corner in (
3.3
) thus identifies with the maximal subgroupoid
\mathrm{Fm}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\subset\mathrm{He}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
of
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
, Def. 2.1.1]
, and the upper left with the subgroupoid
\mathrm{Pn}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\subset\mathrm{Fm}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
spanned by pairs
(x,\xi)
, for
\xi\in\Omega^{\infty}\text{\char 21\relax}(x)
nondegenerate.
\mathrm{Pn}
assembles into a functor
\cat^{p}_{\infty}\to\cal{S}
(note that the restriction to duality-preserving functors in
\cat^{p}_{\infty}
is necessary for this) which is moreover corepresentable (
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
, Prop. 4.1.3]
) and hence preserves limits, and by
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
, Prop. 6.1.8]
filtered colimits.

For an
\infty
-category
K
, denote by
\twar(K)
the twisted arrow category of
K
, with
n
-simplices

\twar_{n}(K)=\Hom_{\sset}(\Delta^{n}\star(\Delta^{n})^{\op},K).

We may equip
\fun(\twar(K),\cal{C})
with a quadratic functor

\text{\char 21\relax}_{K}=\underset{\twar(K)^{\op}}{\lim}\ \text{\char 21\relax},

and restricting this to the subcategory spanned by functors
F
for which the squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(i\to l)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(i\to k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(j\to l)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(j\to k)}

are exact in
\cal{C}
for each map
\Delta^{3}\to K
,
i\to j\to k\to l
, we obtain a hermitian
\infty
-category
(\mathcal{Q}_{K}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}),\text{\char 21\relax}_{K})
. In general, this is not Poincaré, but
upon restricting along the inclusion
\bbDelta\subset\cat_{\infty}
, we obtain a simplicial Poincaré
\infty
-category, denoted
(\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(\cal{C}),\text{\char 21\relax}_{n})
. As in the classical setting, there is a forgetful functor

\mathrm{Pn}(\mathcal{Q}_{n}(\cal{C}),\text{\char 21\relax}_{n})\to\mathcal{Q}_{n}(\cal{C})^{\simeq},

and the Grothendieck-Witt space is equivalent
[
CDH$ˆ+$II
, Cor. 4.1.7]
to the fibre

\cal{GW}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\to|\mathrm{Pn}(\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(\cal{C}),\text{\char 21\relax}_{\bullet})|\to|\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(\cal{C})^{\simeq}|.

Remark 3.1.4
.

As in
[
CDH$ˆ+$II
, App. B]
, it will be convenient to reformulate the above constructions in terms of the hermitian
\mathscr{S}^{e}_{\bullet}
-construction: there is a functor

\iota_{n}:\twar(\Delta^{n})\to\mathrm{Ar}(\Delta^{n}\star(\Delta^{n})^{\op})

sending
(i\leq j)
to
(i\leq j^{\prime})
, where we write the vertices of
\Delta^{n}\star(\Delta^{n})^{\op}
as

(0<\dots<(n-1)<n<n^{\prime}<(n-1)^{\prime}<\dots<0^{\prime}),

which is well known to induce a levelwise equivalence of simplicial stable
\infty
-categories

\iota^{*}_{n}:\mathscr{S}^{e}_{\bullet}(\cal{C})\to\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(\cal{C}).

We equip
\mathscr{S}^{e}_{n}(\cal{C})=\mathscr{S}_{2n+1}(\cal{C})
with a hermitian structure as follows. Write
\mathcal{P}_{n}\subset\mathrm{Ar}(\Delta^{n})
for the subposet on pairs
(i\leq j)
such that
i+j\leq n
. Under the isomorphism

\mathrm{Ar}(\Delta^{2n+1})\cong\mathrm{Ar}(\Delta^{n}\star(\Delta^{n})^{\op}),

\mathcal{P}_{2n+1}
corresponds to the subposet on pairs
i\leq j
or
k\leq l^{\prime}
, for
0\leq k\leq l\leq n
. The poset inclusion
\twar(\Delta^{n})\to\mathrm{Ar}(\Delta^{n}\star(\Delta^{n})^{\op})
thus factors as

\twar(\Delta^{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j_{n}}}{{\subset}}\mathcal{P}_{2n+1}\subset\mathrm{Ar}\Delta^{n}\star(\Delta^{n})^{\op})

and we claim that
j_{n}
is cofinal. Objects of
((i\leq j)\downarrow j_{n})
are maps
(i\leq j)\to(k\leq l^{\prime})
, for some
0\leq k\leq l\leq n
, and likewise objects of
((i\leq j^{\prime})\downarrow j_{n})
are maps
(i\leq j^{\prime})\to(k\leq l^{\prime})
. It is easy to see that the former category has initial object
(i\leq j)\to(i\leq n^{\prime})
, and the latter
(i\leq j^{\prime})=\joinrel=(i\leq j^{\prime})
. The claim follows by Quillen’s theorem A.

For each
n\geq 0
we equip
\mathscr{S}^{e}_{n}(\cal{C})
with quadratic functor

(3.4)

X\mapsto\underset{\twar(\Delta^{n})^{\op}}{\lim}\ \text{\char 21\relax}\circ(\iota_{n}^{*}X)^{\op}\simeq\underset{\mathcal{P}_{2n+1}^{\op}}{\lim}\ \text{\char 21\relax}\circ X^{\op}

obtaining a Poincaré category
(\mathscr{S}_{n}^{e}(\cal{C}),(\iota_{n}^{*})^{*}\text{\char 21\relax}_{n})
tautologically Poincaré equivalent to
(\mathcal{Q}_{n}(\cal{C}),\text{\char 21\relax}_{n})
via
\iota^{*}_{n}
.

3.2.
Categories of Poincaré objects

Given a complicial exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality
(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta,w)
, write
(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)
for the associated Poincaré
\infty
-category of §
2.4
. There is a functor
\wquad(\cal{E},Q,w)\to\mathrm{Pn}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)
induced by naturality of unstraightening: the localisation
\gamma:\cal{E}\to L_{w}(\cal{E})
induces a map of right fibrations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int_{w\cal{E}}\gamma^{*}\mathbf{R}Q_{\mathrm{nd}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pn}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w\cal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{w}(\cal{E})^{\simeq},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}

where the left-hand vertical arrow is classified by the restriction
\gamma^{*}\mathbf{R}Q_{\mathrm{nd}}:w\cal{E}^{\op}\to\cal{S}
, and composing with the map in
\mathrm{RFib}(\cal{E})
induced by the natural transformation
Q_{\mathrm{nd}}\Rightarrow\gamma^{*}\mathbf{R}Q_{\mathrm{nd}}
then gives a map

\wquad(\cal{E},Q,w)\to\mathrm{Pn}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q).

The following proof is a generalisation of the techniques of
[
CDH$ˆ+$II
, Prop. B.2.3]
.

Proposition 3.2.1
.

For
(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta,w)
a complicial exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality, the functor
\wquad(\cal{E},Q,w)\to\mathrm{Pn}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)
induces an equivalence upon realisation.

Proof.

Consider the square

(3.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wquad(\cal{E},Q,w)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pn}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w\cal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{w}(\cal{E})^{\simeq},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}

where each vertical map is a right fibration. By
[
HTT
, Cor. 3.3.4.6]
the homotopy type of the total space of a right fibration is given by the colimit in spaces over the classified functor, and so we have an induced map

\underset{w\cal{E}^{\op}}{\colim}\ Q_{\mathrm{nd}}\to\underset{L_{w}(\cal{E})^{\simeq,\op}}{\colim}\ \mathbf{R}Q_{\mathrm{nd}}.

The functor
w\cal{E}^{\op}\to L_{w}(\cal{E})^{\simeq,\op}
is cofinal by
[
Cis19
, Cor. 7.6.9]
, and so it suffices to consider the map

\underset{w\cal{E}^{\op}}{\colim}\ Q_{\mathrm{nd}}\to\underset{w\cal{E}^{\op}}{\colim}\ \gamma^{*}\mathbf{R}Q_{\mathrm{nd}}

induced by the natural transformation
Q_{\mathrm{nd}}\Rightarrow\gamma^{*}\mathbf{R}Q_{\mathrm{nd}}
. Write as before
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q
resp.
\mathbf{R}Q
for the right derived quadratic
\mathrm{D}(\zz)
-valued functor of
Q
at
w_{\mathrm{Frob}}
resp.
w
. In the case
w
is the class of Frobenius weak equivalences, the identification

(3.6)

\gamma^{*}\mathbf{R}Q_{\mathrm{nd}}(x)=\gamma^{*}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q_{\mathrm{nd}}(x)\simeq Q^{\Delta^{\bullet}}_{\mathrm{nd}}(x)\simeq\underset{J_{x,\mathrm{Frob}}^{\op}}{\colim}\ Q_{\mathrm{nd}}

furnished by the cofinality result Proposition
2.4.7
exhibits the former as the left Kan extension of
Q_{\mathrm{nd}}
along
\gamma
, and the result follows from Proposition
A.1.17
. In the general case, we note that the pullback square (
3.2
) defining
\mathbf{R}Q_{\mathrm{nd}}
is stable under the filtered colimit defining
\pi_{!}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q
, for
\pi:L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})\to L_{w}(\cal{E})
the Verdier projection; accordingly, we have a natural equivalence

\pi_{!}\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q_{\mathrm{nd}}\right)\simeq(\pi_{!}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q)_{\mathrm{nd}}=(\mathbf{R}Q)_{\mathrm{nd}}

and taking filtered colimits of (
3.6
) over
J_{x}^{\op}
, we obtain again that
\mathbf{R}Q_{\mathrm{nd}}
is the right derived functor of
Q_{\mathrm{nd}}
, so again by Proposition
A.1.17
we are done.
∎

3.3.
The hermitian
S_{\bullet}
-construction

We wish to upgrade Proposition
3.2.1
to a levelwise equivalence of simplicial spaces

\left([s]\mapsto|\wquad(S_{2s+1}(\cal{E}),Q_{2s+1},w)|\right)\simeq\mathrm{Pn}(\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(L_{w}(\cal{E})),\mathbf{R}Q_{\bullet}).

To do so, it will suffice to exhibit the latter as the derived Poincaré structure of the former (along with the functoriality argument off
2.6
).

Remark 3.3.1
.

Since the action of
\mathscr{C}(\zz)
on
\cal{E}
is bi-exact, for each
n\geq 0
the pointwise complicial structure on
\fun(\mathrm{Ar}(\Delta^{n}),\cal{E})
preserves the subcategory
S_{n}(\cal{E})
, and the tuple
(S_{n}(\cal{E}),Q_{n},\D_{n},\eta_{n},w)
is a complicial exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality.

Write
\mathscr{S}_{n}(L_{w}(\cal{E})
for the
\infty
-categorical
S_{\bullet}
-construction on
L_{w}(\cal{E})
, i.e. for the subcategory of functors
X:\mathrm{Ar}(\Delta^{n})\to L_{w}(\cal{E})
with
X_{i,i}\simeq 0
for each
0\leq i\leq n
and
X_{i,j}\to X_{i,k}\to X_{j,k}
a fibre sequence in
L_{w}(\cal{E})
for each triple
0\leq i\leq j\leq k\leq n
. Since conflations in
\cal{E}
localise to fibre sequences in
L_{w}(\cal{E})
, the localisation
\cal{E}\to L_{w}(\cal{E})
induces a functor

S_{n}(\cal{E})\to\mathscr{S}_{n}(L_{w}(\cal{E})),

which moreover exhibits
\mathscr{S}_{n}(L_{w}(\cal{E}))
as the localisation of
S_{n}(\cal{E})
at the pointwise weak equivalences, by
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
, Lem. B.2.4]
. Write

(\mathbf{R}Q)_{n}:\mathscr{S}_{n}(L_{w}(\cal{E}))^{\op}\to\mathrm{D}(\zz),\quad X\mapsto\underset{i+j\leq n}{\lim}\ \mathbf{R}Q(X_{i,j}),

and
\mathbf{R}(Q_{n})
for the 2-excisive (right) derived functor of the functor
Q_{n}:S_{n}(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\Ab
of §
3.1.1
with respect to the pointwise weak equivalences in
S_{n}(\cal{E})
. There is a natural transformation
\mathbf{R}(Q_{n})\to(\mathbf{R}Q)_{n}
induced by the universal property of the limit. Note that the inclusion

(3.7)

\mathcal{T}_{n}\subset\{(i,j)\in\mathrm{Ar}(\Delta^{n})\mid i+j\leq n\},

for
\mathcal{T}_{n}
the subposet spanned by
(i,j)
with
i+j=n-1,n
, is cofinal, by an application of Quillen’s theorem A.

Consider for now the case
w=w_{\mathrm{Frob}}
. Then the localisation
S_{n}(\cal{E})\to\mathscr{S}_{n}(L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E}))
factors as

S_{n}(\cal{E})\to L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(S_{n}(\cal{E}))\to\mathscr{S}_{n}(L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})),

for
L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(S_{n}(\cal{E}))
the localisation of
S_{n}(\cal{E})
at the class of Frobenius equivalences internal to the complicial exact category
S_{n}(\cal{E})
. Note that in general, the second localisation is not an equivalence; we claim however that for a class of diagrams, the corresponding map of right derived functors
\mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{Frob}}(Q_{n})\to\mathbf{R}(Q_{n})
is an equivalence, where we write
\mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{Frob}}(Q_{n})
for the derived functor of
Q_{n}
with respect to the internal Frobenius equivalences.

Lemma 3.3.2
.

The natural map
\mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{Frob}}(Q_{n})(X)\to\mathbf{R}(Q_{n})(X)
is an equivalence for each diagram
X\in S_{n}(\cal{E})
in the image of an iterated degeneracy
S_{1}(\cal{E})\to S_{n}(\cal{E})
.

Proof.

Write
\gamma:L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(S_{n}(\cal{E}))\to\mathscr{S}_{n}(L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E}))
for the localisation at the pointwise Frobenius equivalences. Since
\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
acts pointwise on
S_{n}(\cal{E})
, Proposition
2.4.7
implies that
\mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{Frob}}(Q_{n})(X)\simeq(Q_{n})^{\Delta^{\bullet}}(X)
, and the natural map
\mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{Frob}}(Q_{n})(X)\to\mathbf{R}(Q_{n})(X)
identifies with

(3.8)

(Q_{n})^{\Delta^{\bullet}}(X)\to\underset{J_{X}^{\op}}{\colim}\ (Q_{n})^{\Delta^{\bullet}}(X)

for
J_{X}\subset(S_{n}(\cal{E})\downarrow X)
the subcategory of the overcategory spanned by the pointwise Frobenius deflations over
X
. For
0\leq i\leq n+1
,
s^{n}_{i}:[n]\to[1]
for the map in
\bbDelta
sending
0\leq j<i
to
0
, and
i\leq j\leq n
to
1
. Then for
x\in S_{1}(\cal{E})=\cal{E}
, we have

s^{n}_{i}(x)_{j,k}=\begin{cases}x,&0\leq j<i,k\geq i\\
0,&\text{ else,}\end{cases}

with each nonzero map given by
1_{x}
. In the case
X=s^{n}_{i}(x)
for some
x\in\cal{E}
,
(Q_{n})^{\Delta^{\bullet}}(X)
identifies with
\underset{\bbDelta^{\op}}{\colim}\ Q_{n}(s_{i}^{n}(\Delta^{\bullet}x))=\underset{y\in J_{x}^{\op}}{\colim}\ Q_{n}(s_{i}^{n}(y))
, for
J_{x}\subset(\cal{E}\downarrow x)
the subcategory spanned by the trivial Frobenius deflations over
x
, with the map (
3.8
) induced by the functor

(s_{i}^{n})_{x}^{\op}:J_{x}^{\op}\to J_{X}^{\op},\quad(y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\epi}}x)\mapsto(s_{i}^{n}(y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\epi}}s_{i}^{n}(x)).

For
i=0,n+1
,
X
is zero, and the result follows trivially since each of
\mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{Frob}}(Q_{n})
and
\mathbf{R}(Q_{n})
is reduced. For
0<i\leq n
,
(s_{i}^{n})_{x}^{\op}
admits a left adjoint

J_{s_{i}^{n}(x)}^{\op}\to J_{x}^{\op},\quad(Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\epi}}s_{i}^{n}(x))\mapsto(Y_{0,1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\epi}}x),

so that
(s_{i}^{n})_{x}^{\op}
is cofinal.
∎

Proposition 3.3.3
.

For each
n\geq 1
the canonical map
\mathbf{R}(Q_{n})\to(\mathbf{R}Q)_{n}
is an equivalence of quadratic functors.

Proof.

By
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
, Lem. 1.1.25]
, it suffices to exhibit a natural equivalence between connective covers. The case
n=1
holds by the identifications
Q_{1}\cong Q
,
(\mathbf{R}Q)_{1}\simeq\mathbf{R}Q
. Assume firstly that
w=w_{\mathrm{Frob}}
is the class of Frobenius equivalences. Then for
n\geq 2
, a diagram
X\in\mathscr{S}_{n}(L_{w}(\cal{E}))
is determined up to contractible choice by the first row
(X_{0,1}\to X_{0,2}\to\dots\to X_{0,n})
: indeed, writing
\mathscr{I}\subset\mathrm{Ar}(\Delta^{n})
for the subcategory spanned by pairs
(i,j)
with either
i=0
or
i=j
,
X
is the left Kan extension along the inclusion
\mathscr{I}\subset\mathrm{Ar}(\Delta^{n})
of the restriction
X\mid_{\mathscr{I}}
. There is a filtration

X^{1}\hookrightarrow X^{2}\hookrightarrow\dots\hookrightarrow X^{n-1}\hookrightarrow X^{n}=X,

for
X^{k}
the diagram generated by the row
(X_{0,1}\to\dots\to X_{0,k-1}\to X_{0,k}=\joinrel=X_{0,k}=\joinrel=\dots=\joinrel=X_{0,k})
, and for each
1\leq k\leq n-1
the quotient
X^{k,k+1}:=\cofib(X^{k}\hookrightarrow X^{k+1})
satisfies

\left(X^{k,k+1}\right)_{ij}=\begin{cases}X_{k,k+1},&i\leq k,j\geq k+1,\\
0,&\text{ else,}\end{cases}

i.e. is equivalent to
s_{k+1}^{n}(X_{k,k+1})
. Now
\Omega^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}Q)_{n}(X^{k,k+1})=\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{R}Q(X_{k,k+1})=\qdb(X_{k,k+1})
, and similarly
\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{R}(Q_{n})(X^{k,k+1})\simeq\qdb(X_{k,k+1})
, by Lemma
3.3.2
. By 2-excisivity we have identifications, for
1lek\leq n-1
,

\tiny\hskip-56.9055pt\mathrm{fibt}\left(\hbox to139.96pt{\vbox to30.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 69.97989pt\lower-15.27982pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
