\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right).

Noting that the fibre of the lower row in each case is
\Map_{\Delta}(X^{k,k+1},\D(X^{k}))
, we have a map of fibre sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\qdb(X_{k,k+1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fib\left(\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{R}(Q_{n})(X^{k+1})\to\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{R}(Q_{n})(X^{k})\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Delta}(X^{k,k+1},\D(X^{k}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\qdb(X_{k,k+1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fib\left(\Omega^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}Q)_{n}(X^{k+1})\to\Omega^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}Q)_{n}(X^{k})\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Delta}(X^{k,k+1},\D(X^{k})),}

\scriptstyle\simeq

\scriptstyle\simeq

so by induction on
k
, it suffices to show that the map
\Omega^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}Q)_{n}(X^{1})\to\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{R}(Q_{n})(X^{1})
is an equivalence; but
X^{1}=s^{n}_{1}(X_{0,1})
is generated by the row
(X_{0,1}=\joinrel=\dots=\joinrel=X_{0,1})
, and the map in question evaluates to the equivalence

\qdb(X_{0,1})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{R}Q(X_{0,1})

if
n\leq 2
; for
n>2
, each of
\mathbf{R}(Q_{n})(s^{n}_{1}(X_{0,1}))
and
(\mathbf{R}Q)_{n}(s^{n}_{1}(X_{0,1}))
are zero.

For general
w
, write
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q
for the derived functor of
Q
at the Frobenius equivalences, and
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}(Q_{n})
for the derived functor of
Q_{n}
at the pointwise Frobenius equivalences, so that by above
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}(Q_{n})\simeq(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q)_{n}
. Now the derived functor
\mathbf{R}(Q_{n})
of
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}(Q_{n})
at the pointwise weak equivalences is given by the left Kan extension along the localisation

\gamma:\mathscr{S}_{n}(L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E}))\to\mathscr{S}_{n}(L_{w}(\cal{E})),

and we see that since the poset
\mathcal{T}
of (
3.7
) is finite for each
n
, we have equivalences

\mathbf{R}(Q_{n})\simeq\gamma_{!}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}(Q_{n})\simeq\gamma_{!}(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}Q)_{n}\simeq(\mathbf{R}Q)_{n},

since the colimit computing left Kan extension along
\gamma
is filtered.
∎

Propositions
3.2.1
and
3.3.3
then imply:

Corollary 3.3.4
.

Let
(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta,w)
be a complicial exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality. Then for each
n\geq 1
, the map

\wquad(S_{n}(\cal{E}),Q_{n},w_{\mathrm{pt}})\to\mathrm{Pn}(\mathscr{S}_{n}(L_{w}(\cal{E})),(\mathbf{R}Q)_{n})

induces an equivalence upon realisation.

3.4.
Comparing Grothendieck-Witt spaces

We are now ready to prove the main result of the present paper.

Theorem 3.4.1
.

Let
(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta,w)
be a complicial exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality, with associated Poincaré category
(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)
. Then the localisation functor
\cal{E}\to L_{w}(\cal{E})
induces a natural equivalence of Grothendieck-Witt spaces

\cal{GW}(\cal{E},Q)\to\cal{GW}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q).

Proof.

Write
S^{e}_{n}(\cal{E},Q,w)
for the subdivided hermitian
S_{\bullet}
-construction on an exact form category with weak equivalences. Then by Proposition
3.3.4
, for each
n\geq 0
we have equivalences

|\wquad(S^{e}_{n}(\cal{E},Q,w))|\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{Pn}(\mathcal{Q}_{n}(L_{w}(\cal{E})),\mathbf{R}Q_{n}),

assembling into a levelwise equivalence of simplicial spaces

\left([s]\mapsto|\wquad(S_{2s+1}(\cal{E}),Q_{2s+1})|\right)\simeq\mathrm{Pn}(\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(L_{w}(\cal{E})),\mathbf{R}Q_{\bullet}),

and accordingly, since the composite
wS^{e}_{\bullet}(\cal{E})\to\mathscr{S}^{e}_{\bullet}(L_{w}(\cal{E}))^{\simeq}\to\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(L_{w}(\cal{E})^{\simeq})
realises to an equivalence, the induced map on fibres

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{GW}(\cal{E}),Q,w)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces|\wquad(S^{e}_{\bullet}\cal{E},Q^{e}_{\bullet},w)|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces|wS_{\bullet}\cal{E}|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{GW}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|\mathrm{Pn}(\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(L_{w}(\cal{E})),\mathbf{R}Q_{\bullet})|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|(\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(L_{w}(\cal{E}))^{\simeq}|}}

is an equivalence.
∎

In particular, we have the following.

Corollary 3.4.2
.

Suppose
(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta)
is an exact form category with strong duality, with derived Poincaré category
(\db(\cal{E}),\text{\char 21\relax})
; then there is a natural equivalence of Grothendieck-Witt spaces

\cal{GW}(\cal{E},Q)\to\cal{GW}(\db(\cal{E}),\text{\char 21\relax}).

Proof.

We have equivalences

\cal{GW}(\cal{E},Q)\to\cal{GW}(\chb(\cal{E}),Q,\mathbf{qis})\to\cal{GW}(\db(\cal{E}),\text{\char 21\relax}),

where the first is the Gillet-Waldhausen equivalence of
[
Sch24a
]
, and the second follows from Theorem
3.4.1
since
(\chb(\cal{E}),Q,\mathbf{qis},\D,\eta)
is a complicial exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality.
∎

4.
Grothendieck-Witt spectra

In this section we refine the equivalence of Theorem
3.4.1
to a stable equivalence of Grothendieck-Witt spectra. In both the form and Poincaré-categorical setting, the Grothendieck-Witt space admits a canonical delooping into a generally non-connective spectrum
\mathrm{GW}
; we first review the relevant formalism.

4.1.
Delooping
\cal{GW}
of complicial exact form categories

Suppose given a complicial exact from category with weak equivalences and strong duality
(\cal{E},Q,w,\D,\eta)
; for any small category
I
, the functor category
\cal{E}^{I}:=\fun(I,\cal{E})
inherits a pointwise complicial structure via

\otimes^{I}:\mathscr{C}_{\zz}\times\cal{E}^{I}\to\cal{E}^{I},\quad(X,F)\mapsto(i\mapsto X\otimes F(i)),

adjoint to
\otimes\circ(1\times\ev_{\cal{E}}):\mathscr{C}_{\zz}\times\cal{E}^{I}\times I\to\mathscr{C}_{\zz}\times\cal{E}\to\cal{E}
, for
\ev_{I}
the evaluation functor
(F,i)\mapsto F(i)
. For
I=[1]
, the arrow category
\arr(\cal{E}):=\cal{E}^{[1]}
inherits a duality with

\D(x\xrightarrow{f}y)=(\D(y)\xrightarrow{\D(f)}\D(x)),

and double dual identification
\eta_{f}=(\eta_{x},\eta_{y})
. Frobenius contractible objects of
\arr(\cal{E})
are those maps
f
which are retracts of cones on maps, i.e. admitting factorisations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C\otimes u}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C\otimes v}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C\otimes g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{y}}

and there are (generally strict) inclusions
w_{\mathrm{Frob}}\subset w_{\mathrm{Frob}_{\mathrm{pt}}}\subset\arr(\cal{E})
, where the former is the class of Frobenius equivalences internal to
\arr(\cal{E})
, and the latter the class of maps whose image under the source and targets functors are Frobenius equivalences in
\cal{E}
. Write also
w_{\mathrm{pt}}\subset w_{\mathrm{cone}}
for the classes of maps which are pointwise in
w
, resp. such that the induced map on cones
C(f)\to C(g)
is a weak equivalence in
\cal{E}
. We then have a sequence of complicial exact form categories

(\cal{E},Q,w,\D\,\eta)\to(\arr(\cal{E}),Q^{\Delta^{1}},w_{\mathrm{pt}},\D,\eta)\to(\arr(\cal{E}),Q^{\Delta^{1}},w_{\mathrm{cone}},\D,\eta),

where the first functor is
x\mapsto 1_{x}
with duality compatibility the identity
1_{\D(x)}=\D(1_{x})
, and the latter is the change of weak equivalences, with underlying functor the identity. We write
Q^{\Delta^{1}}:\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\Ab
for the functor
Q_{[1]}
of §
3.1.1
, where the poset
[1]
is given the unique strong duality
i\mapsto 1-i
; note the potential clash of notation with the
\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz)
-valued Frobenius derived functor
Q^{\Delta^{\bullet}}
of
Q
. Explicitly, for a map
f:x\to y
in
\cal{E}
, we have

Q^{\Delta^{1}}(f)=\{(\xi,a)\mid\xi\in Q(x),a:x\to\D(y),\rho(\xi)=\D(f)\circ a\},

with
\D_{[1]}(f)=(\D(y)\xrightarrow{\D(f)}\D(x))
, which is strong with respect to
w_{\mathrm{pt}}
. Write
\arr(\cal{E})^{\mathrm{cone}}\subset\arr(\cal{E})
for the subcategory of
w_{\mathrm{cone}}
-acyclic objects. Then the functor
(\cal{E},Q,w,\D,\eta)\to(\arr(\cal{E}),Q^{\Delta^{1}},w_{\mathrm{pt}},\D,\eta)
factors through the inclusion

(\arr(\cal{E})^{\mathrm{cone}},Q^{\Delta^{1}},w_{\mathrm{pt}},\D,\eta)\hookrightarrow(\arr(\cal{E}),Q^{\Delta^{1}},w_{\mathrm{pt}},\D,\eta),

and by
[
Sch24a
, Lem. 11.3]
there is an induced equivalence

\left|\wquad(S_{\bullet}^{e}(\cal{E},Q,w))\right|\xrightarrow{\simeq}\left|\wquad(S_{\bullet}^{e}(\arr(\cal{E})^{\mathrm{cone}},Q^{\Delta^{1}},w_{\mathrm{pt}}))\right|

Write
(\cal{E}^{[n]},Q^{[n]},w)
for the complicial exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality
(\cal{E}^{[n-1]},Q^{[n-1]},w)^{[1]}
for
n\geq 2
, where
(\cal{E},Q,w)^{[1]}=(\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E}),Q^{\Delta^{1}},w_{\mathrm{cone}})
. Then by
[
Sch24a
, Prop. 11.4]
, there are canonical maps for each
n\geq 0
,

(4.1)

\left|\wquad(\mathcal{R}^{(n)}(\cal{E}^{[n]},Q^{[n]},w))\right|\to\Omega\left|\wquad(\mathcal{R}^{(n+1)}(\cal{E}^{[n+1]},Q^{[n+1]},w))\right|,

which for
n\geq 1
are equivalences, and for
n=0
factors as

\left|\wquad(\cal{E},Q,w)\right|\to\cal{GW}(\cal{E},Q,w)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Omega\left|\wquad(\mathcal{R}^{(1)}_{\bullet}(\cal{E}^{[1]},Q^{\Delta^{1}}))\right|.

Here we write
\mathcal{R}^{(n)}_{\bullet}(\cal{E},Q,w)
for the diagonal of the
n^{\mathrm{th}}
iterate of the subdivided
S_{\bullet}
-construction on
(\cal{E},Q,w)
, i.e. the multisimplicial exact form category

(\bbDelta^{\op})^{\times n}\to\formcat,\quad([k_{1}],\dots,[k_{n}])\mapsto S^{e}_{k_{1}}S^{e}_{k_{2}}\dots S^{e}_{k_{n}}(\cal{E},Q,w).

Definition 4.1.1

(
[
Sch24a
, Def. 11.5]
)
.

The Grothendieck-Witt spectrum associated to a complicial exact form category with weak equivalences is the positive
\Omega
-spectrum

\mathrm{GW}(\cal{E},Q,w):=(\left|\wquad(\cal{E},Q,w)\right|,\left|\wquad(\mathcal{R}^{(1)}(\cal{E}^{[1]},Q^{\Delta^{1}})\right|,\left|\wquad(\mathcal{R}^{(2)}(\cal{E}^{[2]},Q^{[2]})\right|,\dots),

with bonding maps those given in (
4.1
). For
n\geq 0
, define the
n
-shifted Grothendieck-Witt spectrum
\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\cal{E},Q,w):=\mathrm{GW}(\cal{E}^{[n]},Q^{[n]},w)
.

4.2.
Delooping
\cal{GW}
of Poincaré
\infty
-categories

Recollection 4.2.1
.

We have the Rezk adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\cal{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cat_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{acat}}

\scriptstyle\vdash

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}

presenting
\cat_{\infty}
of (small)
\infty
-categories as a reflective subcategory of the
\infty
-category of (small) simplicial spaces, with the essential image of the inclusion
N=\Hom_{\cat_{\infty}}(\Delta^{\bullet},-)
the
\infty
-category of complete Segal spaces; see
[
CDH$ˆ+$II
, §2.1]
for a review. We refer to the left adjoint
\mathrm{acat}
as the
associated category
. Given a Segal space
X_{\bullet}
(not necessarily complete) and
x,y\in X_{0}
, we may recover the mapping space
\Hom_{\mathrm{acat}(X)}(x,y)
as the pullback

(4.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\mathrm{acat}(X)}(x,y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{0}\times X_{0},}

\lrcorner

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(d_{1},d_{0})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(x,y)}

by
[
CDH$ˆ+$II
, §2.1]
.

Recall
[
CDH$ˆ+$II
]
that a sequence

(4.3)

(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\xrightarrow{(i,\eta)}(\cal{D},\Phi)\xrightarrow{(p,\vartheta)}(\cal{E},\Psi)

in
\cat^{p}_{\infty}
is a
Poincaré-Verdier sequence
if it is both a fibre and cofibre sequence; in this case the image of (
4.3
) under the canonical functor
\cat^{p}_{\infty}\to\cat^{\mathrm{st}}_{\infty}
is a Verdier sequence, and we say (
4.3
) is
split
if the underlying Verdier sequence splits, i.e.
p
(equivalently
i
) admits both a left and right adjoint. In this situation, we refer to
(i,\eta)
as a (split) Poincaré-Verdier inclusion, and
(p,\vartheta)
as a (split) Poincaré-Verdier projection. A cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cal{C},\Phi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cal{D},\Psi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cal{C}^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\cal{D}^{\prime},\Psi^{\prime})}

is then (split) Poincaré-Verdier if each of the vertical functors are (split) Poincaré-Verdier projections.

A functor
\cal{F}:\cat^{p}_{\infty}\to\cal{S}
is Verdier localising (resp. additive) if it sends Poincaré-Verdier (resp. split Poincaré-Verdier) squares to cartesian squares of spaces, and grouplike if it is additive, and the canonical lift to
\mathrm{Mon}_{\mathbb{E}_{\infty}}(\cal{S})
arising from semi-additivity of
\cat^{p}_{\infty}
takes values in the subcategory of grouplike
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-spaces; see
[
CDH$ˆ+$II
, §1.5]
. Given an additive functor
\cal{F}:\cat^{p}_{\infty}\to\cal{S}
, to any Poincaré category
(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
we obtain an associated simplicial space
\cal{F}\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[1]})
which is Segal for
\cal{F}
additive, and additionally complete if
\cal{F}
preserves limits. The split Poincaré-Verdier sequence

(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\to\mathcal{Q}_{1}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[1]})\xrightarrow{(d_{1},d_{0})}\mathcal{Q}_{0}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[1]})^{\times 2}=(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[1]})^{\times 2},

where the left-hand map is informally given by
x\mapsto(0\leftarrow x\to 0)
with quadratic compatibility the canonical unit equivalence
\text{\char 21\relax}(x)\to\text{\char 21\relax}^{[1]}_{1}(0\leftarrow x\to 0)=\Omega\Sigma\text{\char 21\relax}(x)
, yields upon applying
\cal{F}
a cartesian square of spaces

(4.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{F}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{F}\mathcal{Q}_{1}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[1]})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{F}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[1]})^{\times 2}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0,0)}

Writing
\cob^{\cal{F}}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
for the
\infty
-category associated to the Segal space
\cal{F}\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[1]})
, the square (
4.2
) gives an identification, for
x,y\in\cal{F}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[1]})
,

\Map_{\mathrm{Cob}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})}(x,y)\simeq\fib\left(\cal{F}\mathcal{Q}_{1}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[1]})\to\cal{F}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[1]})^{\times 2}\right),

Accordingly, (
4.4
) induces a map
\cal{F}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Hom_{\cob^{\cal{F}}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})}(0,0)\to\Omega\left|\cob^{\cal{F}}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\right|
. For additive
\cal{F}
, the functor
(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\mapsto\left|\cob^{\cal{F}}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\right|
is again additive, and the assignment
\cal{F}\mapsto\left|\cob^{\cal{F}}(-)\right|
assembles into an endofunctor
\left|\cob^{(-)}(-)\right|
of
\fun^{\mathrm{add}}(\cat_{\infty}^{p},\cal{S})
. By
[
CDH$ˆ+$II
, Th. 3.3.4]
,
\left|\cob^{\cal{F}}(-)\right|
is a model for the suspension of
\cal{F}
in
\fun^{\mathrm{add}}(\cat_{\infty}^{p},\cal{S})
, and for
\cal{F}
group-like, the map
\cal{F}\to\Omega\left|\cob^{\cal{F}}(-)\right|
is an equivalence; moreover, the latter map exhibits the target as the group completion of
\cal{F}
. For
n\geq 0
, write

\mathcal{Q}^{(n)}_{\bullet,\dots,\bullet}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}):(\bbDelta^{\op})^{\times n}\to\cat^{p}_{\infty},\quad([k_{1}],\dots,[k_{n}])\mapsto\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}}\mathcal{Q}_{k_{2}}\dots\mathcal{Q}_{k_{n}}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})

for the
n
-simplicial hermitian
\mathcal{Q}
-construction on
(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
,
\mathcal{Q}^{(n)}_{\bullet}
for the diagonal, and
\mathrm{Cob}_{n}^{\cal{F}}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}):=\cal{F}\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}^{(n)}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[n]})
. We have
\mathrm{Cob}^{\cal{F}}_{1}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})=\cal{F}\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[1]})
, and canonical identifications

\left|\mathrm{Cob}_{1}^{|\mathrm{Cob}_{n}^{\cal{F}}(-)|}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\right|\simeq\left|\cal{F}\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}^{(n+1)}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[n+1]})\right|=\left|\mathrm{Cob}_{n+1}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\right|.

Then for additive
\cal{F}
, by
[
CDH$ˆ+$II
, Prop. 3.4.5]
, the maps

\left|\mathrm{Cob}_{n}^{\cal{F}}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\right|\to\Omega\left|\mathrm{Cob}_{n+1}^{\cal{F}}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\right|

adjoint to the equivalences
\Sigma|\mathrm{Cob}_{n}^{\cal{F}}(-)|\simeq|\mathrm{Cob}_{1}^{|\mathrm{Cob}_{n}^{\cal{F}}(-)|}|
are equivalences for
n\geq 1
, and we write
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ob}^{\cal{F}}(-)
for the associated positive
\Omega
-spectrum, with
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ob}^{\cal{F}}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})_{n}=\left|\mathrm{Cob}^{\cal{F}}_{n}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\right|
. For
\cal{F}
grouplike,
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ob}^{\cal{F}}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
is an
\Omega
-spectrum, and the group completion map
\cal{F}\to\cal{F}^{\mathrm{grp}}
induces the spectrification map

\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ob}^{\cal{F}}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\to\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ob}^{\cal{F}^{\mathrm{grp}}}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})

for each
(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\in\cat^{p}_{\infty}
. Recall that
\mathrm{Pn}:\cat^{p}_{\infty}\to\cal{S}
is additive, with group completion
\cal{GW}
, and write
\mathrm{Cob}_{n}:=\mathrm{Cob}_{n}^{\mathrm{Pn}}
.

Definition 4.2.2
.

The Grothendieck-Witt pre-spectrum associated to a Poincaré category
(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
is the positive
\Omega
-spectrum

\mathrm{GW}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}):=(\left|\mathrm{Cob}_{0}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\right|,\left|\mathrm{Cob}_{1}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\right|,\left|\mathrm{Cob}_{2}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\right|,\dots),

and for
n\in\zz
we set
\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}):=\mathrm{GW}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[n]})
, the
n
-shifted Grothendieck-Witt pre-spectrum. As above, the map
\mathrm{Pn}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})=\left|\mathrm{Cob}_{0}^{\mathrm{Pn}}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\right|\to\Omega\left|\mathrm{Cob}_{1}^{\mathrm{Pn}}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\right|\simeq\cal{GW}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
is the universal map exhibiting the functor
\cal{GW}:\cat_{\infty}^{p}\to\cal{S}
as the group completion of
\mathrm{Pn}
.

4.3.
Comparing Grothendieck-Witt spectra

Fix a complicial exact form category with weak equivalences and strong duality
(\cal{E},Q,w,\D,\eta)
, with derived Poincaré category
(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)
. By
[
Cis19
, Th. 7.6.17]
, the functor
\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E})\to\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E}))
induces an equivalence
L_{w_{\mathrm{pt}}}(\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E}))\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E}))
, and we write
(\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E})),\mathbf{R}(Q^{\Delta^{1}}))
for the derived Poincaré category associated to
(\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E}),Q^{\Delta^{1}},w_{\mathrm{pt}},\D,\eta)
. Given a Poincaré category
(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
, the arrow category
\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{C})
can be equipped with a quadratic functor
\text{\char 21\relax}_{\mathrm{ar}}
, defined by the cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{\char 21\relax}_{\mathrm{ar}}(x\xrightarrow{f}y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{\char 21\relax}(x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{\text{\char 21\relax}}(y,x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{\text{\char 21\relax}}(x,x).}

The pair
(\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{C}),\text{\char 21\relax}_{\mathrm{ar}})
is Poincaré if
(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
is
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
, §2.4]
.

Lemma 4.3.1
.

There is a natural Poincaré equivalence

(\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E})),\mathbf{R}(Q^{\Delta^{1}}))\simeq(\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E})),(\mathbf{R}Q)_{\mathrm{ar}}).

Proof.

Assume for now that
w=w_{\mathrm{Frob}}
is the class of Frobenius equivalences in
\cal{E}
. Then by the results of §
2
, there are equivalences

\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{R}Q\simeq\qdb,\quad\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{Frob}}(Q^{\Delta^{1}})\simeq(Q^{\Delta^{1}})^{\Delta^{\bullet}},

where we write
\mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{Frob}}(Q^{\Delta^{1}})
for the right derived quadratic functor of
Q^{\Delta^{1}}
at the Frobenius equivalences internal to
\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E})
. Write
\gamma:L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E}))\to\mathrm{Ar}(L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E}))
for the Dwyer-Kan localisation at the pointwise Frobenius equivalences, so that
\mathbf{R}(Q^{\Delta^{1}})(f)=\gamma_{!}\mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{Frob}}(Q^{\Delta^{1}})(f)
for a map
x\xrightarrow{f}y
. Writing
J_{f}\subset(\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E})\downarrow f)
for the full subcategory of the slice spanned by (pointwise) trivial Frobenius deflations over
f
, by the pointwise formula for left Kan extensions we have

\gamma_{!}(Q^{\Delta^{1}})^{\Delta^{\bullet}}(f)\simeq\underset{g\in J_{f}^{\op}}{\colim}\ (Q^{\Delta^{1}})^{\Delta^{\bullet}}(g).

We claim (cf. Lemma
3.8
) that the canonical maps

(Q^{\Delta^{1}})^{\Delta^{\bullet}}(0\to x)\to\gamma_{!}(Q^{\Delta^{1}})^{\Delta^{\bullet}}(0\to x),\quad(Q^{\Delta^{1}})^{\Delta^{\bullet}}(1_{x})\to\gamma_{!}(Q^{\Delta^{1}})^{\Delta^{\bullet}}(1_{x})

are equivalences in
\mathrm{D}_{\geq 0}(\zz)
. By Lemma
2.4.7
we have

\displaystyle(Q^{\Delta^{1}})^{\Delta^{\bullet}}(0\to x)

\displaystyle=Q^{1}(0\to\Delta^{\bullet}x)\simeq\underset{y\in J_{x}^{\op}}{\colim}\ Q^{1}(0\to y),

\displaystyle(Q^{\Delta^{1}})^{\Delta^{\bullet}}(1_{x})

\displaystyle=Q^{1}(1_{\Delta^{\bullet}x})\simeq\underset{y\in J_{x}^{\op}}{\colim}\ Q^{1}(1_{y}).

Now we have adjoint pairs

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\epi}}x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0\to y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\epi}}x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J_{0\to x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\epi}}x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(y\to z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\epi}}x),}

\scriptstyle\vdash

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p:y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\epi}}x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((p,p):(1_{y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\epi}}1_{x})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J_{1_{x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p:y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\epi}}x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((p,q):(y\to z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\epi}}1_{x}),}

\scriptstyle\vdash

and since any right adjoint is cofinal, the claim follows upon taking opposite categories. Note accordingly that

\Omega^{\infty}\gamma_{!}(Q^{\Delta^{1}})^{\Delta^{\bullet}}(0\to x)\simeq Q^{\Delta^{1}}(0\to\Delta^{\bullet}x)=0,\quad\Omega^{\infty}\gamma_{!}(Q^{\Delta^{1}})^{\Delta^{\bullet}}(1_{x})=Q^{\Delta^{1}}(1_{\Delta^{\bullet}x})=\qdb(x).

Now for
f\in\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E})
, consider the map of fibre sequences in
\mathrm{Ar}(L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E}))

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

furnished by
f
. Since
\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{R}(Q^{\Delta^{1}})
is 2-excisive, there is an induced equivalence of total fibres

\hskip-28.45274pt\small\mathrm{fibt}\left[\hbox to208.45pt{\vbox to53.51pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 104.22552pt\lower-26.75526pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

and upon passing to connective covers and commuting fibres, we obtain a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{R}(Q^{\Delta^{1}})(f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{R}Q(x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Delta}(y,\D(x))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{\Delta}(x,\D(x)),}

and a natural equivalence
\Omega^{\infty}\mathbf{R}(Q^{\Delta^{1}})(f)\simeq\Omega^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}Q)_{\mathrm{ar}}(f)
, which by Proposition
2.5.7
promotes to an equivalence of quadratic
\mathrm{D}(\zz)
-valued functors
\mathbf{R}(Q^{\Delta^{1}})\simeq(\mathbf{R}Q)_{\mathrm{ar}}
on
\mathrm{Ar}(L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E}))
. To conclude for general
w\subset\cal{E}
, we simply note that the definition of
(\mathbf{R}Q)_{\mathrm{ar}}
is stable under filtered colimits, so the derived functor
\mathbf{R}(Q^{\Delta^{1}})
of
Q^{\Delta^{1}}
at the pointwise (
w
-)weak equivalences is naturally equivalent to
(\mathbf{R}Q)_{\mathrm{ar}}
on
\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E}))\simeq L_{w_{\mathrm{pt}}}(\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E}))
.
∎

Recall that associated to a Poincaré category
(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
is the
metabolic
Poincaré category
\mathrm{Met}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
, with underlying stable category
\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{C}):=\fun(\Delta^{1},\cal{C})
and quadratic functor

\text{\char 21\relax}_{\mathrm{met}}(f):=\fib(\text{\char 21\relax}(y)\xrightarrow{\text{\char 21\relax}(f)}\text{\char 21\relax}(x)),

inducing a duality

(x\xrightarrow{f}y)\mapsto(\fib(\D(y)\to\D(x))\to\D(y)).

\mathrm{Met}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
fits into the metabolic (split) Poincaré-Verdier sequence

(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\to\mathrm{Met}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[1]})\to(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[1]}),

with the first functor informally given by
x\mapsto(x\to 0)
, and the second by
(x\to y)\mapsto y
, with quadratic compatibilities

\text{\char 21\relax}(x)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\fib(0\to\Sigma\text{\char 21\relax}(x)),\quad\Sigma\fib\left(\text{\char 21\relax}(y)\xrightarrow{\text{\char 21\relax}(f)}\text{\char 21\relax}(x)\right)\to\Sigma\text{\char 21\relax}(y).

Writing
S_{2}\cal{C}\subset\fun(\Delta^{2},\cal{C})
for the full subcategory on fibre-cofibre sequences in
\cal{C}
, by
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
, Lem. 2.4 .5]
the zig-zag of equivalences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{2}(\cal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(x\to y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(x\to y\to z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(y\to z)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}

induces an equivalence of restricted quadratic functors

d_{2}^{*}\text{\char 21\relax}_{\mathrm{ar}}\simeq d_{0}^{*}\text{\char 21\relax}_{\mathrm{met}}^{[1]}.

We thus have an equivalence of Poincaré-Verdier inclusions

(4.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E})),(\mathbf{R}Q)_{\mathrm{ar}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Met}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)^{[1]},}

\scriptstyle\simeq

with the right vertical arrow informally given by
(x\xrightarrow{f}y)\mapsto(y\to\cofib(f))
. The functor
L_{w}(\cal{E})\to\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E}))
,
x\mapsto 1_{x}
clearly lands in the stable subcategory of cone-acyclic maps, and participates in the adjoint pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{w}(\cal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x\mapsto 1_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

\scriptstyle\vdash

where
s
is the source functor. Since
\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E}))^{\mathrm{cone}}
is simply the full subcategory spanned by the equivalences in
L_{w}(\cal{E})
, this restricts to an equivalence

L_{w}(\cal{E})\simeq\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E}))^{\mathrm{cone}},

and accordingly the upper Poincaré-Verdier inclusion of (
4.5
) is equivalent to

(\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E})^{\mathrm{cone}}),(\mathbf{R}Q)_{\mathrm{ar}}\mid_{\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E})^{\mathrm{cone}})})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),(\mathbf{R}Q)_{\mathrm{ar}}).

Writing
\pi:\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E}))\to L_{\mathrm{cone}}(\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E}))
for the localisation at
w_{\mathrm{cone}}
, we obtain:

Corollary 4.3.2
.

There is a natural Poincaré equivalence

\left(L_{\mathrm{cone}}(\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E})),\pi_{!}(\mathbf{R}(Q^{\Delta^{1}}))\right)\simeq\left(L_{w}(\cal{E}),(\mathbf{R}Q)^{[1]}\right).

Corollary 4.3.3
.

Let
(\cal{E},Q,w,\D,\eta)
be a complicial exact form category with weak equivalences, with derived Poincaré category
(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\text{\char 21\relax})
. Then for each
n\geq 0
, there is a natural equivalence of spaces

\left|\wquad\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}^{(n)}(\cal{E},Q,w)^{[n]}\right|\xrightarrow{\simeq}\left|\mathrm{Cob}_{n}(L_{w}(\cal{E},\mathbf{R}Q))\right|

Proof.

We show inductively that the complicial exact form category
(\cal{E},Q,w)^{[n]}
has derived Poincaré category
(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q^{[n]})
: the case
n=0
holds by definition. By Corollary
4.3.2
, for
(\cal{E},Q,w,\D,\eta)
any complicial exact form category with weak equivalences with derived Poincaré category
(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)
, the derived Poincaré category of
(\cal{E},Q,w)^{[1]}
is (Poincaré-equivalent to)
\left(L_{w}(\cal{E}),(\mathbf{R}Q)^{[1]}\right)
. Since for any
n\geq 1
and any indices
k_{,}\dots,k_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that
\mathcal{R}^{(n)}_{k_{1},\dots,k_{n}}(\cal{E},Q,w)
is levelwise a complicial exact form category with weak equivalences, for
n\geq 1
, there are natural equivalences

\displaystyle\left|\wquad\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}^{(n)}(\cal{E}^{[n]},Q^{[n]},w^{[n]})\right|

\displaystyle\simeq\left|\wquad\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}^{(n)}(\cal{E}^{[n-1]},Q^{[n-1]},w^{[n-1]})^{[1]}\right|

\displaystyle\simeq\left|\mathrm{Pn}\mathcal{Q}^{(n)}_{\bullet}\left(L_{w_{n-1}}(\cal{E}^{[n-1]}),(\mathbf{R}Q_{n-1})^{[1]}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\simeq\left|\mathrm{Pn}\mathcal{Q}^{(n)}_{\bullet}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),(\mathbf{R}Q)^{[n]})\right|=\left|\mathrm{Cob}_{n}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)\right|,

where the second equivalence uses the natural identification

|\wquad\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}(\cal{E},Q,w)|\simeq|\mathrm{Pn}\mathscr{S}^{e}_{\bullet}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)|\simeq|\mathrm{Pn}\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)|

for
(\cal{E},Q,w)
any complicial exact form category with weak equivalences.
∎

For
n\geq 0
, write
\gamma_{n}:\left|\wquad\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}^{(n)}(\cal{E},Q,w)^{[n]}\right|\to\Omega\left|\wquad\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}^{(n+1)}(\cal{E},Q,w)^{[n+1]}\right|
for the bonding maps constructed in
[
Sch24a
]
, and
\delta_{n}:\left|\cob_{n}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)\right|\to\Omega\left|\cob_{n+1}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)\right|
for those of
[
CDH$ˆ+$II
]
. We make the following recollection: given a Poincaré category
(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
, the shifted metabolic sequence

(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[-1]})\to\mathrm{Met}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\to(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})

is split Poincaré-Verdier, and is hence taken by the additive functor
\left|\cob(-)\right|
to the fibre sequence

\left|\cob(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[-1]})\right|\to\left|\cob(\mathrm{Met}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}))\right|\to\left|\cob(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\right|.

By
[
CDH$ˆ+$II
, Prop. 3.1.10]
, the boundary map
\Omega\left|\cob(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\right|\xrightarrow{\partial}\left|\cob(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[-1]})\right|
fits into the diagram

(4.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pn}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\left|\cob(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\right|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left|\cob(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[-1]})\right|,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}

with the left-hand map the bonding map induced by the identification
\mathrm{Pn}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\simeq\Hom_{\cob(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})}(0,0)
, and the right-hand by the inclusion of
0
-simplices
(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax}^{[-1]})=\mathcal{Q}_{0}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})\to\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
. Now, recall from
[
Sch24a
, §11]
that associated to a complicial exact form category with weak equivalences
(\cal{E},Q,w,\D,\eta)
is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wquad(\cal{E},Q,w)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wquad\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}(\cal{E},Q,w)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wquad\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}(\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E}),Q^{\Delta^{1}},w_{\mathrm{pt}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces wS_{\bullet}\cal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wquad\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}(\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E})^{\mathrm{cone}},Q^{\Delta^{1}},w_{\mathrm{cone}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wquad\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}(\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E}),Q^{\Delta^{1}},w_{\mathrm{cone}}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}

where the functor
\iota
is the inclusion of
0
-simplices, and
I
is levelwise given by the form functor

(\cal{E},Q,w)\to(\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E}),Q^{\Delta^{1}},w),\quad x\mapsto 1_{x}.

That the map

(4.7)

\left|\wquad(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}(\arr(\cal{E}),Q^{\Delta^{1}},w_{\mathrm{pt}}))\right|\to\left|wS_{\bullet}\cal{E}\right|

is a weak equivalence follows ultimately for additivity for hermitian
K
-theory of exact form categories (with weak equivalences and strong duality)
[
Sch24a
, Th. 3.1 (8.1)]
, and moreover, the maps in these theorems are natural in the input form category, as seen from inspection of the proof of
[
Sch24a
, Th. 3.1]
. The map (
4.7
) thus admits a functorial homotopy inverse, and accordingly the rectangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wquad(\cal{E},Q,w)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wquad\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}(\cal{E},Q,w)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wquad\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}(\mathrm{Ar}(\cal{E}),Q^{\Delta^{1}},w_{\mathrm{pt}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

commutes up to natural homotopy. Taking derived Poincaré categories, we obtain a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pn}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pn}\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pn}\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(\mathrm{Ar}(L_{w}(\cal{E})),(\mathbf{R}Q)_{\mathrm{ar}})^{[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pn}\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),(\mathbf{R}Q)^{[1]})}

of simplicial Poincaré categories commuting up to canonical homotopy, which we further identify via (
4.5
) with

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pn}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pn}\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pn}\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}\mathrm{Met}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)^{[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pn}\mathcal{Q}_{\bullet}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),(\mathbf{R}Q)^{[1]}),}

where the lower square encodes the fibre sequence associated to the metabolic Poincaré-Verdier sequence. Taking realisations, we obtain the essentially unique map of spaces
\gamma_{0}:\left|\mathrm{Pn}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)\right|\to\Omega\left|\mathrm{Cob}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)\right|
rendering the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Pn}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\left|\cob(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)\right|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left|\cob(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q^{[-1]})\right|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left|\cob(\mathrm{Met}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)\right|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left|\cob(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)\right|}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}

commutative, in which the top square encodes the rotated fibre sequence
\Omega\left|\cob(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q^{[-1]})\right|\to\left|\cob(\mathrm{Met}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)\right|\to\left|\cob(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)\right|
. The map
\gamma_{0}
is determined up to contractible choice by the universal property of the limit, and accordingly is homotopic (up to contractible choice) to
\delta_{0}
. The same argument applies replacing
(\cal{E},Q,w,\D,\eta)
with the multisimplicial complicial exact form category with weak equivalences
\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}^{(n)}(\cal{E},Q,w)^{[n]}
, and accordingly we obtain (a contractible space of) homotopies between the bonding maps
\gamma_{n}
and
\delta_{n}
.

Remark 4.3.4
.

Suppose given a sequence of pointed spaces
(X_{n},*)_{n\geq 0}
, contractible subspaces
i_{n}:C_{n}\subset\Map_{*}((X_{n},*),\Omega(X_{n+1},*))
, for
\Map_{*}
the pointed mapping space in the closed symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\cal{S}_{*}^{\wedge}
of pointed spaces with the smash product. The maps
i_{n}
adjoin to pointed maps
j_{n}:(X_{n},*)\to\Map_{*}((C_{n})_{+},\Omega(X_{n+1},*)
, and we set
\widetilde{X}_{n}:=\Map_{*}(\bigwedge_{i<n}(C_{n})_{+},(X_{n},*))
. We then have bonding maps
\alpha_{n}:\widetilde{X}_{n}\to\Omega\widetilde{X}_{n+1}
given by the composite

\Map_{*}(\bigwedge_{i<n}(C_{n})_{+},(X_{n},*))\xrightarrow{[1,j_{n}]}\Map_{*}(\bigwedge_{i<n}(C_{i})_{+},\Map_{*}((C_{n})_{+},\Omega(X_{n+1},*)))\simeq\Omega\Map_{*}(\bigwedge_{i\leq n}(C_{i})_{+},(X_{n+1},*)).

This map is contravariantly functorial in the maps
i_{n}
: given for each
n\geq 0
contractible subspaces
C_{n},C^{\prime}_{n}
and a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{*}((X_{n},*),\Omega(X_{n+1},*))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{n},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{n}}

we have a commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{*}(\bigwedge_{i<n}(C_{i})_{+},(X_{n},*))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{*}(\bigwedge_{i\leq n}(C_{i})_{+},\Map_{*}((C_{n})_{+},\Omega(X_{n+1},*)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{*}(\bigwedge_{i<n}(C^{\prime}_{i})_{+},(X_{n},*))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Map_{*}(\bigwedge_{i\leq n}(C^{\prime}_{i})_{+},\Map_{*}((C_{n})_{+},\Omega(X_{n+1},*))),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[1,j_{n}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\left(\bigwedge_{i<n}(f_{i})_{+}\right),[(f_{n})_{+},1]\right]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\left(\bigwedge_{i<n}(f_{i})_{+}\right),1\right]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[1,j^{\prime}_{n}]}

and accordingly we have maps of (pre-)spectra
(\widetilde{X}_{n},\alpha_{n})_{n}\to(\widetilde{X}^{\prime}_{n},\alpha^{\prime}_{n})_{n}
. In the case
C^{\prime\prime}_{n},C^{\prime}_{n}\to C_{n}
are simply the inclusions of points corresponding to choices of bonding maps
\gamma_{n},\delta_{n}
, we obtain a canonical zig-zag of weak equivalences of (pre-)spectra

(X_{n},\gamma_{n})_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\leftarrow}}(\widetilde{X}_{n},\alpha_{n})_{n}\xrightarrow{\simeq}(X_{n},\delta_{n})_{n}.

The above discussion and Proposition
4.3.3
imply:

Theorem 4.3.5
.

Suppose given a complicial exact form category with weak equivalences
(\cal{E},Q,w,\D,\eta)
, with derived Poincaré category
(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q)
. Then the localisation
\cal{E}\to L_{w}(\cal{E})
induces a weak equivalence of Grothendieck-Witt spectra

\mathrm{GW}(\cal{E},Q,w)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{GW}(L_{w}(\cal{E}),\mathbf{R}Q).

5.
Genuine symmetric Poincaré structures

In this section we give a treatment of the formalism of genuine Poincaré structures of
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
]
and
[
CHN24
]
, showing in cases of interest that these are precisely the derived Poincaré structures of exact form categories.

5.1.
Bounded derived Poincaré categories

Given an exact category with strong duality
(\cal{E},\D,\eta)
embedding exactly into the complicial exact category with weak equivalences and strong duality
(\chb(\cal{E}),\mathbf{qis},\D,\eta)
, we have an associated duality-preserving Verdier-projection

(\kb(\cal{E}),\D,\eta)\to(\db(\cal{E}),\D^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}),

where
\D^{\prime}
is the left Kan extension of the composite
\pi\circ\D
along the localisation
\pi^{\op}:\kb(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\db(\cal{E})^{\op}
, which exists since
\D:\chb(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\chb(\cal{E})
preserves acyclic complexes. Writing
(\kb(\cal{E}),\text{\char 21\relax}_{\oplus}^{s})
and
(\db(\cal{E}),\text{\char 21\relax}^{s})
for the corresponding symmetric Poincaré-structures, one may ask whether in general this promotes to a Poincaré-Verdier projection

(\kb(\cal{E}),\text{\char 21\relax}_{\oplus}^{s})\to(\db(\cal{E}),\text{\char 21\relax}^{s}),

or equivalently
[
CDH$ˆ+$II
, Cor. 1.1.6]
if the induced map
\text{\char 21\relax}_{\oplus}^{s}\to\pi^{*}\text{\char 21\relax}^{s}
exhibits
\text{\char 21\relax}^{s}
as the left Kan extension of
\text{\char 21\relax}_{\oplus}^{s}
along
\pi^{\op}
. This will not be the case for a general duality-preserving Verdier quotient of stable
\infty
-categories, since a priori there is no reason for the commutation of the filtered colimit computing the mapping spectra
\map_{\cal{D}}(-,-)
and the formation of
C_{2}
-homotopy fixed points. The situation at hand is however sufficiently finitary enough to permit this.

Remark 5.1.1
.

(i)

The below essentially amounts to showing that
\kb(\cal{E})\to\db(\cal{E})
is a bounded Karoubi projection of
\dperf(\zz)
-linear stable
\infty
-categories in the sense of
[
CHN24
, §4]
. Since we only need that mapping spectra in
\kb(\cal{E})
can be computed (up to equivalence) as mapping complexes in the dg-category
\chb(\cal{E})
, we dispense with the full
\dperf(\zz)
-linearity; it is the case however that any complicial exact category localises to a
\zz
-linear stable
\infty
-category, giving us an honest tensoring over
\dperf(\zz)
.

(ii)

For the remainder of the section we assume
\cal{E}
is
weakly idempotent complete
(i.e., every retract admits a kernel). Since by
[
Sch24a
, Lem. 10.6]
for any exact form category with strong duality
(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta)
the weak idempotent completion
\cal{E}\to\cal{E}^{\flat}
enhances to an exact form functor

(\cal{E},Q,\D,\eta)\to(\cal{E}^{\flat},Q^{\flat},\D^{\flat},\eta^{\flat})

of exact form categories with strong duality, such that the induced map on Grothendieck-Witt spaces is an equivalence, this constitutes no real loss of generality. The category
\chb(\cal{E}^{\flat})
is also idempotent complete, and the induced functor
\chb(\cal{E})\to\chb(\cal{E}^{\flat})
promotes to an exact functor of complicial exact categories with weak equivalences and duality.

Lemma 5.1.2
.

For
\cal{E}
weakly idempotent complete, the functor
(\kb(\cal{E}),\text{\char 21\relax}_{\oplus}^{s})\to(\db(\cal{E}),\text{\char 21\relax}^{s})
is a Poincaré-Verdier projection.

Proof.

Write
\gamma:\chb(\cal{E})\to\kb(\cal{E})
for the Dwyer-Kan localisation at the Frobenius (chain homotopy) equivalences, and
\pi:\kb(\cal{E})\to\db(\cal{E})
for the Verdier projection. The pointwise formula for left Kan extensions gives for
x\in\kb(\cal{E})
an identification

(\pi_{!}\text{\char 21\relax}_{\oplus}^{s})(\pi(x))\simeq\colim\left(\cal{I}_{x}^{\op}\to\kb(\cal{E})\xrightarrow{\text{\char 21\relax}_{\oplus}^{s}}\mathrm{D}(\zz)\right)=\underset{y\in\cal{I}_{x}^{\op}}{\colim}\ \hom_{\kb(\cal{E})}(y,\D(y))^{\hct},

for
\cal{I}_{x}\subset(\kb(\cal{E})\downarrow x)
the full subcategory of quasi-isomorphisms over
x
. Now by
[
HA
, Prop. 1.3.5.21]
, the standard
t
-structure on
\mathrm{D}(\zz)
is right-separated with
\mathrm{D}_{\leq 0}(\zz)
stable under filtered colimits, so by
[
CHN24
, Cor. 4.2.10]
we see that filtered colimits of uniformly bounded above diagrams in
\mathrm{D}(\zz)
commute with finite type limits, i.e. limits indexed by simplicial sets
K
with finitely many non-degenerate simplices in each degree. We observe that the duality
\D:\chb(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\chb(\cal{E})
restricts to an equivalence
\mathrm{Ch}_{[a,b]}(\cal{E})^{\op}\simeq\mathrm{Ch}_{[-b,-a]}(\cal{E})
for each
b\geq a
, and accordingly for
y\in\mathrm{Ch}_{[a,b]}(\cal{E})
, the mapping complex
\underline{\Hom}(y,\D(y))
is concentrated in degrees
[-2b,-2a]
. The mapping spectrum
\map_{\kb(\cal{E})}(\gamma(y),\D(\gamma(y))
hence lies in
\mathrm{D}_{[-2b,-2a]}(\zz)=\mathrm{D}_{\geq-2b}(\zz)\cap\mathrm{D}_{\leq-2a}(\zz)
, with respect to the standard
t
-structure on
\mathrm{D}(\zz)
. Given a family of complexes
\{y_{j}\}_{j\in\cal{J}}\subset\kb(\cal{E})
, uniformly bounding the coconnectivity of the family
\{\hom_{\kb(\cal{E})}(y_{j},\D(y_{j}))\}_{j\in\cal{J}}
then amounts to uniformly bounding the connectivity of the
y_{j}
.

Suppose
x\in\mathrm{Ch}_{\geq 0,\mathrm{b}}(\cal{E})
; the general case follows by shifting. Then a quasi-isomorphism over
\gamma(x)
in
\kb(\cal{E})
is represented by some map
f:y\to x
in
\chb(\cal{E})
with acyclic cone; since
\cal{E}
is weakly idempotent complete, any acyclic complex is strictly acyclic
[
Büh10
, Prop. 10.14]
, and accordingly
\mathrm{cone}(f)
admits factorisations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{1}\oplus y_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{0}\oplus y_{-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}p_{0}\amsamp q_{0}\end{pmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}d_{1}\amsamp-f_{0}\\
0\amsamp-d_{0}\end{pmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}p_{-1}\amsamp q_{-1}\end{pmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}0\amsamp-d_{-1}\end{pmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}i_{0}\\
j_{0}\end{pmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{-1}}

with the sequence
w_{0}\mono x_{0}\oplus y_{-1}\epi w_{-1}
a conflation in
\cal{E}
. Since
j_{-1}
is monic, the map
q_{-1}
is necessarily zero, and accordingly for
y_{\geq-2}
the complex
\dots\to y_{0}\to y_{-1}\epi w_{-1}\to 0\to\dots
, the composite map
y_{\geq-2}\mono y\to x
has acyclic cone given by

\dots\to x_{1}\oplus y_{0}\to x_{0}\oplus y_{-1}\epi w_{-2}\to 0\dots

(we note that this truncation is by no means sharp). Write
j_{x}:\cal{I}_{x}^{\geq-2}\hookrightarrow\cal{I}_{x}
for the full subcategory on quasi-isomorphisms
y\to x
in
\kb(\cal{E})
with
y
concentrated in degrees
\geq-2
. Then we claim the inclusion
j_{x}^{\op}
is cofinal: since
\kb(\cal{E})
admits finite limits and colimits, that the same is true for the iterated comma categories
((y,f)\downarrow j_{x}^{\op})=(j_{x}\downarrow(y,f))^{\op}
follows from the stability of the class of quasi-isomorphisms under pushout and pullbacks. These are hence contractible as soon as they are inhabited; but this follows from above, and Quillen’s theorem A completes the proof.
∎

5.2.
Orientations on the derived category

Suppose given a small exact category with duality
(\cal{E},\D,\eta)
, and write
(\cal{E}_{\oplus},\D,\eta)
for the underlying additive category with duality. The exact duality-preserving functor
(\cal{E}_{\oplus},\D,\eta)\to(\cal{E},\D,\eta)
induces a duality-preserving functor of stable
\infty
-categories with perfect duality
(\kb(\cal{E}),\D)\to(\db(\cal{E}),\D)
. For
\cal{C}
a stable
\infty
-category with category of
\mathrm{Ind}
-objects
\mathrm{Ind}(\cal{C})
, we refer to the data of a
t
-structure on
\mathrm{Ind}(\cal{C})
as an
orientation
, following
[
CHN24
]
.

For
x\in\cal{E}
, write
P_{x}
for the set of deflations over
x
. Then the families
(P_{x})_{x\in\cal{E}}
generate a Grothendieck topology on
x
, for which a presheaf of abelian groups
\cal{E}^{\op}\to\Ab
is a sheaf precisely if it sends conflations in
\cal{E}
to left-exact sequences of abelian groups
[
Büh10
, App. A]
. Write
\elex:=\mathrm{Sh}^{\Ab}(\cal{E})
for the Gabriel-Quillen abelian hull of such sheaves, a Grothendieck abelian category with system of compact generators given by the image under the Yoneda embedding of
\cal{E}
; in the case
\cal{E}
is split exact, these generators are additionally projective. With the above Grothendieck topology,
\cal{E}
is an additive
\infty
-site in the sense of
[
Pst23
, §2]
; since for each
x,y\in\cal{E}
the sequence
x\mono x\oplus y\epi y
is a conflation, each sheaf is necessarily additive, i.e. the map
X(x\oplus y)\to X(x)\oplus X(y)
induced by the canonical inclusions is an equivalence. Following
[
Pst23
]
, we call an additive presheaf
spherical
. By the universal properties of
\kb(\cal{E})
and
\db(\cal{E})
, there are identifications

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}(\kb(\cal{E}))=\fun^{\mathrm{ex}}(\kb(\cal{E})^{\op},\Sp)\simeq\fun^{\oplus}(\cal{E}^{\op},\Sp)=\cal{P}^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}(\db(\cal{E}))=\fun^{\mathrm{ex}}(\db(\cal{E})^{\op},\Sp)\simeq\fun^{\mathrm{ex}}(\cal{E}^{\op},\Sp)=\sh^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E}),

where we use
[
Pst23
, Th. 2.8]
to identify spherical sheaves of spectra on
\cal{E}
with those spherical presheaves sending conflations to fibre sequences of spectra. Note that the Yoneda embedding
\cal{E}_{\oplus}\hookrightarrow\cal{E}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{lex}}=\fun^{\oplus}(\cal{E}^{\op},\Ab)
induces by
[
Pst23
, Lem. 2.61]
an equivalence

\cal{P}^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{D}(\cal{E}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{lex}})

between the the
\infty
-category of spherical presheaves of spectra on
\cal{E}
and unbounded derived
\infty
-category of
\cal{E}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{lex}}
. Given a spherical sheaf of spectra
X
on
\cal{E}
, write
\pi_{n}^{\dagger}(X):\cal{E}^{\op}\to\Ab
for the sheaf associated to the assignment
c\mapsto\pi_{n}X(c)
; call
X
connective if
\pi_{n}^{\dagger}(X)=0
for each
n<0
, and coconnective if the sheaf of spaces
\Omega^{\infty}X
given by postcomposition with
\Omega^{\infty}:\Sp\to\cal{S}
is discrete. By
[
Pst23
, Prop. 2.16]
, the full subcategories
\sh^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})_{\geq 0}
and
\sh^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})_{\leq 0}
of connective and coconnective spherical sheaves respectively define a
t
-structure on
\sh^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})
, and we denote by
\tau_{\geq 0}
,
\tau_{\leq 0}
the respective truncation functors.

Lemma 5.2.1
.

The truncation functor
\tau_{\geq 0}:\sh^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})\to\sh^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})_{\geq 0}
commutes with filtered colimits of spherical sheaves.

Proof.

By
[
Pst23
, Th. 2.8]
, filtered colimits of spherical sheaves are computed levelwise. Writing
\sh_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})
for the
\infty
-category of spherical sheaves of spaces on
\cal{E}
, there is by
[
Pst23
, Prop. 2.19]
an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sh_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sh^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{\infty}}

\scriptstyle\vdash

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}}

with
\Omega^{\infty}
computed levelwise, and with
\Sigma^{\infty}
fully faithful with essential image the connective aisle
\sh^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})_{\geq 0}
. Write

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sh_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sh^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})_{\geq 0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}

\scriptstyle\vdash

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega}

for the induced adjoint equivalence; then by uniqueness of adjoints, there is a natural equivalence
\omega\tau_{\geq 0}\simeq\Omega^{\infty}
, and since
\Omega^{\infty}
commutes with filtered colimits, we are done.
∎

5.3.
Genuine symmetric structures

For a hermitian category
(\cal{C},\text{\char 21\relax})
, recall that the first excisive approximation
P_{1}\text{\char 21\relax}=\colim_{n}\Omega^{n}\text{\char 21\relax}\Sigma^{n}
is computed as the cofibre

\left[\Delta^{*}B_{\text{\char 21\relax}}\right]_{\hct}\to\text{\char 21\relax}\to P_{1}\text{\char 21\relax},

since by
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
, Prop. 1.1.13]
this cofibre is 1-excisive,
P_{1}
is left exact, and by
[
CDH$ˆ+$I
, Lem. 1.3.1]
the functor
\left[\Delta^{*}B_{\text{\char 21\relax}}\right]_{\hct}
has vanishing 1-excisive approximation. We may view
P_{1}\text{\char 21\relax}
as an object of
\mathrm{Ind}(\cal{C})=\fun^{\mathrm{lex}}(\cal{C}^{\op},\cal{S})\simeq\fun^{\mathrm{ex}}(\cal{C}^{\op},\Sp)
, and if
\cal{C}
is equipped with an orientation
(\mathrm{Ind}_{\geq 0}(\cal{C}),\mathrm{Ind}_{\leq 0}(\cal{C}))
, we say that
˘
is
m
-connective
if
P_{1}\text{\char 21\relax}\in\mathrm{Ind}(\cal{C})
is
m
-connective. By
[
CHN24
, Lem. 3.3.1]
, the inclusion
\fun_{\geq m}^{\mathrm{q}}(\cal{C})\subset\fun^{\mathrm{q}}(\cal{C})
of
m
-connective quadratic functors with respect to this orientation admits a right adjoint
\text{\char 21\relax}\mapsto\text{\char 21\relax}\underset{P_{1}\text{\char 21\relax}}{\times}\tau_{\geq m}P_{1}\text{\char 21\relax}
, where the connective cover is taken in
\mathrm{Ind}(\cal{C})
. In the case of an exact category with duality
(\cal{E},\D,\eta)
, write
Q^{s}:\cal{E}^{\op}\to\Ab
for the symmetric forms functor, and
\text{\char 21\relax}_{\kb}^{s}:\db(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\Sp
for the (homotopy) symmetric Poincaré structure on
\db(\cal{E})
, with linear part

\Lambda^{s}:=\hom_{\db(\cal{E})}(-,\D(-))^{\tct}.

Write
(\sh_{\Sigma}^{\Sp}(\cal{E})_{\geq 0},\sh^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})_{\leq 0})
for the
t
-structure of the previous section, and for
m\in\zz
write
\text{\char 21\relax}^{\geq m}:=\text{\char 21\relax}^{s}\underset{\tau_{\geq m}\Lambda^{s}}{\times}\Lambda^{s}
for the associated truncated hermitian structure.

Lemma 5.3.1
.

For
(\cal{E},\D,\eta)
a weakly idempotent complete- exact category with strong duality, the genuine symmetric Poincaré structure
\text{\char 21\relax}^{\geq 0}
coincides with the derived quadratic functor of
Q^{s}
on
\db(\cal{E})
.

Proof.

We first treat the split-exact case. For
\cal{E}=\cal{E}_{\oplus}
an additive category, the
t
-structure on
\cal{P}_{\Sigma}^{\Sp}(\cal{E})
has connective part those spherical presheaves of spectra factoring through
\Sp_{\geq 0}\subset\Sp
, and since postcomposition with the truncation functor associated with the canonical
t
-structure on spectra sends spherical presheaves of spectra to spherical presheaves of connective spectra, we have for each
x\in\cal{E}
and
\cal{F}\in\cal{P}_{\Sigma}^{\Sp}(\cal{E})
that
(\tau_{\geq 0}\cal{F})(x)=\tau_{\geq 0}(\cal{F}(x))
. Accordingly, the cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{\char 21\relax}^{\geq 0}(x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\geq 0}\hom_{\kb(\cal{E})}(x,\D(x))^{\tct}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hom_{\kb(\cal{E})}(x,\D(x))^{\hct}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hom_{\kb(\cal{E})}(x,\D(x))^{\tct}}

induces a fibre sequence

\text{\char 21\relax}^{\geq 0}(x)\to\hom_{\kb(\cal{E})}(x,\D(x))^{\hct}\to\tau_{\leq-1}\hom_{\kb(\cal{E})}(x,\D(x))^{\tct}

for each
x\in\cal{E}\subset\kb(\cal{E})
. Since the duality
\D:\kb(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\kb(\cal{E})
preserves discrete complexes and
\cal{E}\subset\kb(\cal{E})
is fully faithful,
\hom_{\kb(\cal{E})}(x,\D(x))^{\hct}
is coconnective, and the associated long exact sequence on homotopy groups implies that
\text{\char 21\relax}^{\geq 0}(x)
is concentrated in degree 0, with

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\text{\char 21\relax}^{\geq 0}(x)

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{0}\text{\char 21\relax}^{s}(x)

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\left[\hom_{\kb(\cal{E})}(x,\D(x))^{\hct}\right]

\displaystyle\cong\left[\pi_{0}\hom_{\kb(\cal{E})}(x,\D(x))\right]^{\fct}

\displaystyle\cong\Hom_{\cal{E}}(x,\D(x))^{\fct}=Q^{s}(x),

and we conclude by
[
BGMN22
, Th. 2.19]
.

For
\cal{E}
a general exact category, write
\text{\char 21\relax}^{s}_{\oplus}:\kb(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\Sp
and
\text{\char 21\relax}^{s}:\db(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\Sp
for the respective symmetric Poincaré structures, with truncations
\text{\char 21\relax}^{\geq 0}_{\oplus}
and
\text{\char 21\relax}^{\geq 0}
. The statement for general
\cal{E}
follows from the claim that the left Kan extension of
\tau_{\geq 0}\text{\char 21\relax}_{\oplus}
along the Verdier projection is equivalent to
\text{\char 21\relax}^{\geq 0}
. By
[
HTT
, Rem. 6.2.2.12]
, the sheafification functor
(-)^{\dagger}:\cal{P}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E}^{\Sp})\to\sh_{\Sigma}^{\Sp}(\cal{E})
is computed as a filtered colimit

\cal{F}^{\dagger}(x)=\underset{y\epi x}{\colim}\ \cal{F}(y),

and coincides with left Kan extension along
\pi^{\op}
under the identifications
\cal{P}_{\Sigma}^{\Sp}(\cal{E})\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\kb(\cal{E}))
,
\sh^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\db(\cal{E}))
; we abuse notation by writing
\pi_{!}
for each of these functors below. We claim that the square

(5.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{P}_{\Sigma}^{\Sp}(\cal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sh_{\Sigma}^{\Sp}(\cal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{P}^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})_{\geq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sh^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})_{\geq 0},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\geq 0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\geq 0}}

commutes. Since
\Omega^{\infty}:\Sp\to\cal{S}
commutes with filtered colimits, the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{P}_{\Sigma}^{\Sp}(\cal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sh_{\Sigma}^{\Sp}(\cal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{P}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sh_{\Sigma}(\cal{E}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}}

commutes, where
\Omega^{\infty}
is computed levelwise; but by
[
Pst23
, Prop. 2.19]
, the adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sh_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sh^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}}

\scriptstyle\vdash

where again
\Omega^{\infty}
is computed levelwise, has
\Sigma^{\infty}
fully faithful, with essential image the connective aisle
\sh^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})_{\geq 0}
. We thus have a factorisation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{P}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{P}_{\Sigma}^{\Sp}(\cal{E})_{\geq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{P}^{\Sp}_{\Sigma}(\cal{E}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{\infty}}

and so by uniqueness of adjoints,
\Omega^{\infty}
is homotopic up to postcomposition with an equivalence to the truncation
\tau_{\geq 0}
and in particular preserves filtered colimits, so that (
5.1
) commutes. We thus have for each excisive functor
\Lambda:\kb(\cal{E})^{\op}\to\Sp
that
\pi_{!}\tau_{\geq 0}\Lambda^{s}\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}\pi_{!}\Lambda
, and in particular

\pi_{!}\tau_{\geq 0}P_{1}\text{\char 21\relax}_{\oplus}^{s}\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}\pi_{!}P_{1}\text{\char 21\relax}_{\oplus}^{s}\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}P_{1}\text{\char 21\relax}^{s},

where the last equivalence follows from Lemma
5.1.2
and the fact that
P_{1}
is a left adjoint. In particular, the fibre sequence

\pi_{!}(\left(\hom_{\kb(\cal{E})}(-,\D(-))_{\hct}\right)(x)\to\pi_{!}\left(\hom_{\kb(\cal{E})}(-,\D(-))^{\hct}\right)(x)\to\pi_{!}\left(\hom_{\kb(\cal{E})}(-,\D(-))^{\tct}\right)(x)

coincides for each
x\in\db(\cal{E})
with

\hom_{\db(\cal{E})}(x,\D(x))_{\hct}\to\hom_{\db(\cal{E})}(x,\D(x))^{\hct}\to\hom_{\db(\cal{E})}(x,\D(x))^{\tct}=P_{1}\text{\char 21\relax}^{s}(x).

Now the cartesian square defining
\text{\char 21\relax}_{\oplus}^{\geq 0}
is stable under the filtered colimit computing left Kan extension along the Verdier projection
\kb(\cal{E})\to\db(\cal{E})
, and so

\pi_{!}\text{\char 21\relax}_{\oplus}^{\geq 0}=\pi_{!}\left[\text{\char 21\relax}_{\oplus}^{s}\underset{\Lambda_{\oplus}^{s}}{\times}\tau_{\geq 0}\Lambda^{s}_{\oplus}\right]\simeq\left[\pi_{!}\text{\char 21\relax}_{\oplus}^{s}\right]\underset{\left[\pi_{!}P_{1}\text{\char 21\relax}_{\oplus}^{s}\right]}{\times}(\left[\pi_{!}\tau_{\geq 0}P_{1}\text{\char 21\relax}^{s}_{\oplus}\right]\simeq\text{\char 21\relax}^{\geq 0},

and we are done.
∎

Appendix A
Deriving exact categories

A.1.
Complicial exact categories

In this section we review some results on complicial exact categories
[
Sch11
, Def. 3.2.2]
. These are similar in spirit to the complicial biWaldhausen categories of Thomason-Trobaugh
[
TT90
]
; the notable example is that of (sub)categories of chain complexes over an exact category. We start with following observation.

Any additive category admits a canonical action of the closed symmetric monoidal category of finitely generated free
\zz
-modules
\projz
, encoding the direct sum. If
\cal{E}
is exact, this promotes by virtue of the fact that conflations are stable under direct sum to a biexact functor
\otimes:\projz\times\cal{E}\to\cal{E}
(with respect to the split-exact structure on
\projz
). It is natural to ask whether this action extends via the canonical inclusion
\projz\hookrightarrow\chb(\projz)
to an action of the symmetric monoidal category of bounded complexes over
\projz
.

Write
\mathscr{C}_{\zz}:=\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{b}}(\projz)
. Then the canonical examples of such an extension is the action
\mathscr{C}_{\zz}\times\chb(\cal{E})\to\chb(\cal{E})
, defined as follows: for bounded complexes
K_{*}\in\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
and
X_{*}\in\chb(\cal{E})
,
K\otimes X
is the complex given in degree
n
by

(K\otimes X)_{n}=\bigoplus_{i}K_{i}\otimes X_{n-i},

with differential given on the summand
K_{i}\otimes X_{n-i}
by
d^{K}_{i}\otimes 1_{X_{n-i}}+(-1)^{i}\otimes d^{X}_{n-i}
. Given maps of complexes
(A_{*},f_{*}):(K_{*},X_{*})\to(L_{*},Y_{*})
, where
A_{*}
is degreewise the matrix
(a^{n}_{\alpha\beta})_{\alpha\beta}\in\mathrm{Mat}_{l_{n}\times k_{n}}(\zz)
, the induced map
K\otimes X\to L\otimes Y
is given in degree
n
on the summand
K_{i}\otimes X_{n-i}=X_{n-i}^{\oplus k_{i}}
by the matrix
(a^{i}_{\alpha\beta}\cdot f_{n-i}):X^{\oplus k_{i}}\to Y^{\oplus l_{i}}
. Since the tensor product of complexes preserves connectivity, this also restricts to an action
\mathscr{C}_{\zz,\geq 0}\times\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{b},\geq 0}(\cal{E})\to\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathrm{b},\geq 0}(\cal{E})
.

Now the category
\chb(\cal{E})
inherits a canonical exact structure from
\cal{E}
in which conflations are defined degreewise, and moreover comes equipped with a notion of weak equivalence: a complex
X_{*}
is said to be strictly acyclic if the differentials admit factorisations
d_{n}:X_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p_{n}}}{{\epi}}Z_{n-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{n-1}}}{{\mono}}X_{n-1}
, such that the sequences
Z_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{n}}}{{\mono}}X_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p_{n}}}{{\epi}}Z_{n-1}
are conflations in
\cal{E}
;
X_{*}
is then acyclic if chain homotopy equivalent to a strictly acyclic complex. If
\cal{E}
is weakly idempotent complete (i.e. any retract admits a kernel), the classes of acyclic and strictly acyclic complexes coincide
[
Büh10
, Prop. 10.14]
. A map of complexes
X_{*}\to Y_{*}
is then said to be a
quasi-isomorphism
if its mapping cone is acyclic. The degreewise conflations and quasi-isomorphisms equip
\chb(\cal{E})
with the structure of an exact category with weak equivalences, and the tensor product
\otimes:(\mathscr{C}_{\zz},\mathbf{ch.htp.})\times(\chb(\cal{E}),\mathbf{qis})\to(\chb(\cal{E}),\mathbf{qis})
is an exact functor of such categories, where
\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
is equipped with the degreewise split-exact structure and the class of corresponding quasi-isomorphisms, which coincides with the chain homotopy equivalences. The symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
is compatible with this structure in the sense that the tensor product preserves chain homotopy equivalences in each variable and is biexact; we call such a category a symmetric monoidal exact category with weak equivalences, and note that the inclusion
\projz\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
is monoidal.

Notation A.1.1
.

Write
\mathbb{1}:=\zz[0]
for the unit of the monoidal structure on
\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
, and
C
for the complex
0\to\zz=\joinrel=\zz\to 0
in degrees
[0,1]
, sitting in the conflation
\mathbb{1}\mono C\epi T
. Dually, for
P:=C[-1]
, we have a conflation
\Omega\mono T\epi\mathbb{1}
. Write
[-,-]
for the mapping complex in
\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
, given degreewise by

[X,Y]_{n}=\prod_{i}\Hom_{\projz}(X_{i},Y_{i+n}),

with differential
\partial(f)=d\circ f-(-1)^{|f|}f\circ d
. The closed symmetric monoidal structure
(\mathscr{C}_{\zz},\otimes,[-,-],\mathbb{1})
induces for each
n\in\zz
a strong duality
K\mapsto[K,\mathbb{1}[n]]
, with double dual identification
\mathrm{can}:K\to[[K,\mathbb{1}[n]],\mathbb{1}[n]]
the adjunct of the evaluation map
\mathrm{ev}_{\mathbb{1}[n]}:[K,\mathbb{1}[n]]\otimes K\to\mathbb{1}[n]
.

Definition A.1.2
.

A
complicial
structure on an exact category with weak equivalences
(\cal{E},w)
is the data of a biexact action of the symmetric monoidal exact category with weak equivalences
(\mathscr{C}_{\zz},\mathbf{ch.htp},\otimes,\mathbb{1})
, i.e. a functor

\otimes:\mathscr{C}_{\zz}\times\cal{E}\to\cal{E}

which is exact in each variable as a functor of exact categories with weak equivalences, and is coherently unital and associative in the sense of
[
Gra76
]
. In particular, we note that given maps
f_{*}:K_{*}\to L_{*}
in
\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
and
p:x\to y
in
\cal{E}
, the induced map
f\otimes p:K\otimes x\to L\otimes y
lies in
w
as soon as
f
is a chain homotopy equivalence and
p\in w
. An exact functor of complicial exact categories with weak equivalences
f:(\cal{E},w)\to(\cal{E}^{\prime},w^{\prime})
is simply an exact functor of the underlying exact categories with weak equivalences;
f
is said to be complicial exact if it is compatible with the action of
\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
in the sense that there is a natural isomorphism rendering the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}_{\zz}\times\cal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}_{\zz}\times\cal{E}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{E}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes^{\prime}}

commutative, satisfying the constraints of
[
Gra76
]
. We will generally abuse terminology by referring to an exact category with a complicial structure as a complicial exact category. For
K\in\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
and
x\in\cal{E}
, we may write
Kx:=K\otimes x
.

We may refine the above definition to the case where
(\cal{E},w,\D,\eta)
is an exact category with weak equivalences and duality; recall that this implies in particular that
\D:\cal{E}^{\op}\to\cal{E}
is an exact functor of exact categories with weak equivalences.

Definition A.1.3
.

An exact category with weak equivalences and duality
(\cal{E},w,\D,\eta)
is complicial if the tensor product
\otimes:\mathscr{C}_{\zz}\times\cal{E}\to\cal{E}
refines to a duality-preserving form functor of exact categories with duality, i.e. if the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}_{\zz}^{\op}\times\cal{E}^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}_{\zz}\times\cal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{E}^{\op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[-,\mathbb{1}]\otimes\D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes^{\op}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\D}

commutes up to indicated natural isomorphism. That this is the case for
\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
with complicial structure induced by monoidality, with duality
X\mapsto[X,\mathbb{1}]
, follows from the natural isomorphism

[X,A]\otimes[Y,B]\to[X\otimes Y,A\otimes B],\quad f\otimes g\mapsto(f\otimes g:x\otimes y\mapsto(-1)^{|x|||g|}f(x)\otimes g(y)).

Remark A.1.4
.

We note that the opposite of a complicial exact category is complicial exact, with structure map furnished by the duality in
\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
:

(A.1)

\mathscr{C}_{\zz}\times\cal{E}^{\op}\xrightarrow{[-,\mathbb{1}]\times 1_{\cal{E}}}\mathscr{C}_{\zz}^{\op}\times\cal{E}^{\op}\xrightarrow{\otimes^{\op}}\cal{E}^{\op}.

Recollection A.1.5
.

For
\cal{E}
any exact category, an object
z\in\cal{E}
is said to be injective if for each inflation
i:x\mono y
, the map
i^{*}:\Hom_{\cal{E}}(y,z)\to\Hom_{\cal{E}}(x,z)
is a surjection; dually,
z
is projective if for any deflation
p:y\epi x
, the map
p_{*}\Hom_{\cal{E}}(z,y)\to\Hom_{\cal{E}}(z,x)
is a surjection.
\cal{E}
is said to have enough injectives (projectives) if each
x\in\cal{E}
admits an inflation
x\mono I_{x}
into an injective object (a deflation
P_{x}\epi x
from a projective object). A
Frobenius exact category
is then an eaxct category with enough injectives and projectives, and in which the classes of injectives and projectives coincide.

Recall
[
Sch11
, Lem. A.2.16]
that any complicial exact category has an underlying Frobenius exact category, as follows: declare an inflation
i:x\mono y
to be Frobenius if for each
u\in\cal{E}
, the map
i^{*}:\Hom_{\cal{E}}(y,C\otimes u)\to\Hom_{\cal{E}}(x,C\otimes u)
is surjective. Dually, a deflation
p:y\epi x
is Frobenius if for each
u\in\cal{E}
,
p_{*}:\Hom_{\cal{E}}(C\otimes u,y)\to\Hom_{\cal{E}}(C\otimes u,x)
is a surjection. The Frobenius inflations and deflations equip
\cal{E}
with a Frobenius exact structure, in which an object is injective-projective if it is a retract of
C\otimes u
for some
u
.

Definition A.1.6
.

Given a Frobenius exact category
\cal{E}
, call morphisms
f,g:x\to y
Frobenius homotopic if their difference factors through an injective-projective object, and call a map
f:x\to y
a Frobenius equivalence if it admits a two-sided inverse up to Frobenius homotopy. Frobenius homotopy then defines an equivalence relation on
\Hom_{\cal{E}}(x,y)
for each
x,y\in\cal{E}
, and the corresponding quotient category is the
stable category

\underline{\cal{E}}
. This can be equipped with a canonical triangulated structure
[
Sch11
, §2.14]
, as follows: choose for each
x
an inflation into an injective-projective
x\mono I_{x}
; then a triangle in
\underline{\cal{E}}
is distinguished if it is isomorphic to one of the form

x\xrightarrow{f}y\to I_{x}\cup_{x}y\to I_{x}/x.

Example A.1.7
.

The Frobenius exact structure on
\chb(\cal{E})
arising from its complicial exact structure comprises the degreewise-split monomorphisms and epimorphisms. A map of complexes is then a Frobenius equivalence if and only if it is a chain homotopy equivalence.

Remark A.1.8
.

Any complicial exact category
\cal{E}
supports a notion of simplicial homotopy: call maps
f,g:x\to y
simplicially homotopic if there exists a map
H:\Delta^{1}\otimes x\to y
rendering the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{1}x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

commutative; here we write
\Delta^{1}
for the image of the standard 1-simplex under the functor
N_{*}\zz[-]:\sset\to\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
, and
d^{0},d^{1}
respectively for the maps induced by the inclusions
\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}:\zz[0]=\Delta^{0}\to\Delta^{1}
. Maps
f,g:x\to y
are simplicially homotopic if and only if they are Frobenius homotopic: a Frobenius homotopy
f-g:x\to Cu\to y
, factors by injectivity of
Cu
over the Frobenius inflation
x\mono Cx
induced by the map
\mathbb{1}\mono C
. With reference to the retract diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zz}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zz}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zz}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zz\oplus\zz}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zz}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zz}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}-1\\
1\end{pmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}-1\\
1\end{pmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}-1\amsamp 0\end{pmatrix}}

we then have a simplicial homotopy

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Cx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g-f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

and hence from
f
to
g
by the evident additivity of simplicial homotopy. Conversely, given a simplicial homotopy
f\sim g
, the difference
d^{0}-d^{1}:x\to\Delta^{1}x
factors through the inclusion
Cx\to\Delta^{1}x
, and we have a factorisation
g-f:x\xrightarrow{d^{0}-d^{1}}Cx\xrightarrow{Hi}y
.

Let
(\cal{E},w)
be an exact category with weak equivalences. An object
x\in\cal{E}
is said to be (
w
-)
acyclic
if the unique map
0\to x
is a weak equivalence. By
[
Sch24a
, Lem. 7.1]
, an inflation
x\mono y
is a weak equivalence if and only if its cokernel is acyclic, and dually a deflation
y\epi x
is a weak equivalence if and only if its kernel is acyclic (we call such inflations or deflations (
w
-)trivial). Accordingly, the full subcategory
\cal{E}^{w}\subset\cal{E}
of acyclic objects is exact and closed under extensions in
\cal{E}
.

Write
(w)\mathrm{Inf}
and
(w)\mathrm{Def}
for the classes of (trivial) inflations and deflations in
\cal{E}
. Then the pairs
(w\mathrm{Inf},\mathrm{Def})
and
(\mathrm{Inf},w\mathrm{Def})
each form a functorial factorisation system on
\cal{E}
: given a map
f:x\to y
, we have a commutative diagram

(A.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Cx\oplus y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x\oplus Py}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}\iota_{x}\\
f\end{pmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}1_{x}\\
0\end{pmatrix}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}0\amsamp 1_{y}\end{pmatrix}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}0\amsamp\pi_{y}\end{pmatrix}}

where we write
\iota_{x}:x\mono Cx
and
\pi_{y}:Py\epi y
for the maps induced respectively by
\mathbb{1}\mono C
and
P\epi\mathbb{1}
in
\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
. Note that each vertical map is a Frobenius equivalence by contractibility of the respective kernel or cokernel.

Remark A.1.9
.

For a map
f:x\to y
in
\cal{E}
, define the mapping cone
C(f)
as the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Cx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(f),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{x}}

and dually define the mapping cocone
F(f)
as the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Py}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

From the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Cx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Cx\oplus y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

\scriptstyle\sim

in which the horizontal and vertical sequences are conflations, we see that
f\in w\cal{E}
if and only if
C(f)
is acyclic, and dualising, if and only if
F(f)
is acyclic. Given a Frobenius equivalence
f:x\to y
, it follows from the map of distinguished triangles

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Cx\cong 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tx,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

in
\underline{\cal{E}}
that
C(f)\cong 0
in
\underline{\cal{E}}
, so that
C(f)
is a retract of some
Cu
in
\cal{E}
; but
Cu
is acyclic since the map
0\to Cu
is the tensor product
(0\to C)\otimes 1_{u}
of weak equivalences, and
w\cal{E}
is closed under retracts, so that any Frobenius equivalence lies in
w\cal{E}
.

Writing
w_{\mathrm{Frob}}\subset w
for the Frobenius equivalences, the pair
(\cal{E},w_{\mathrm{Frob}})
is again an exact category with weak equivalences: given a Frobenius equivalence
f:x\to z
, and a deflation
q:z^{\prime}\epi z
, we have by virtue of the factorisation system
(w\mathrm{Inf},\mathrm{Def})
a diagram of cartesian squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

with the left-hand square bicartesian.
\ker(p^{\prime})\cong\ker(p)
and
\coker(i^{\prime})\cong\coker(i)
are thus Frobenius contractible, so that the pullback
f^{\prime}=p^{\prime}i^{\prime}
of
f
along
q
is a Frobenius equivalence. The argument for stability under pushouts is dual, and the closure of
w_{\mathrm{Frob}}
follows from the closure of
\cal{E}^{w}
under retracts and the fact that
f:x\to y
is a Frobenius equivalence precisely when its cone is acyclic. We accordingly have exact inclusions

(\cal{E}_{\mathrm{Frob}},w_{\mathrm{Frob}})\subset(\cal{E},w_{\mathrm{Frob}})\subset(\cal{E},w)

of exact categories with weak equivalences.

For
n\geq 0
, write
\Delta^{n}\in\mathscr{C}_{\zz}
for image of the standard
n
-simplex under the composite
\sset\xrightarrow{\zz[-]}\sab\xrightarrow{N}\mathrm{Ch}_{\geq 0}(\zz)
. Then we have a mapping space bifunctor

\cal{E}^{\op}\times\cal{E}\to\sab,\quad(x,y)\mapsto\Map_{\Delta}(x,y):=\Hom_{\cal{E}}(\Delta^{\bullet}x,y),

equipping
\cal{E}
with a simplicial enrichment such that the resulting simplicial category
\cal{E}_{\Delta}
is fibrant in the Bergner model structure. By Remark
A.1.8
, we have
\pi_{0}\Map_{\Delta}(x,y)\cong\Hom_{\underline{\cal{E}}}(x,y)
, and writing
L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})
for the Dwyer-Kan localisation at the Frobenius equivalences, we have a factorisation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\Delta}(\cal{E}_{\Delta}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

with
f
clearly essentially surjective, and the functor
\cal{E}\to N_{\Delta}(\cal{E}_{\Delta})
induced by the inclusion of
0
-simplices. Now it follows from Proposition
2.4.6
that
\Map_{\Delta}(-,-)
sends Frobenius equivalences in each variable to homotopy equivalences. Applying Proposition
2.4.7
to the functors
\Map_{\Delta}(-,y)
and
\Map_{\Delta}(x,-)
yields natural equivalences

\Map_{\Delta}(x,y)\simeq\underset{x^{\prime}\in J^{\op}_{x,\mathrm{Frob}}}{\colim}\ \Hom(x^{\prime},y),

where we write
J_{x,\mathrm{Frob}}\subset(\cal{E}\downarrow x)
for the full subcategory spanned by trivial Frobenius deflations over
x
which lie in
w_{\mathrm{Frob}}
, and the colimit is take in
\cal{S}
. Since the maps
\Hom_{\cal{E}}(x,y)\to\Map_{L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})}(\gamma(x),\gamma(y))
and
\Hom_{\cal{E}}(x,y)\to\Map_{\Delta}(x,y)
each identify with the canonical inclusions

\Hom_{\cal{E}}(x,y)\to\underset{x^{\prime}\in J_{x,\mathrm{Frob}}^{\op}}{\colim}\ \Hom_{\cal{E}}(x^{\prime},y),

we see that the functor
f
is fully faithful, i.e.
N_{\Delta}(\cal{E}_{\Delta})
is a model for the Dwyer-Kan localisation
L_{\mathrm{Frob}}(\cal{E})
.

Proposition A.1.10
.

The Dwyer-Kan localisation of any complicial exact catgeory with weak equivalences is a stable
\infty
-category. Moreover, an exact functor between such induces an exact functor of stable
\infty
-categories.

Proof.

This is in spirit the same as
[
BC20
, Prop. 2.7]
, but we provide the details. We first note that
(\cal{E},w)
is an
\infty
-category of fibrant objects (Lemma
2.4.2
), so that by
[
Cis19
, Prop. 7.5.6]
, the localisation
L_{w}(\cal{E})
admits finite limits, and the localisation
\gamma:\cal{E}\to L_{w}(\cal{E})
is left exact. The same argument applied to the complicial exact category with weak equivalences
(\cal{E}^{\op},w^{\op})
furnishes us with finite colimits, and since
0
is both initial and final,
L_{w}(\cal{E})
is in addition pointed.

Now each map in
L_{w}(\cal{E})
is up to equivalence the image of an inflation under
\gamma
